# Stedding: the plan to finish what exists

> **Owner decisions, 2026-09-26.** These replace the questions at the end of section 2.
>
> 1. Horizontal tab mode stays. Do not remove or hide the toggle. WIN-1, SPC-3, CMD-2 and TAB-18 are fixes: switching either way must not crash, Stedding's views are torn down and rebuilt with the strip, menu ids are range-checked, and Spaces, pins and folders keep working in horizontal mode.
> 2. Peek is off by default until it is rebuilt.
> 3. The Space sleep timer defaults to Never until sleep is gated.
> 4. Every cut listed in section 2 is approved.
> 5. ⌘9 selects the last row.
> 6. Collapsing the folder that holds the active tab keeps that tab showing under the header. No tab switch.
> 7. Deleting the active Space switches to its neighbour first. Only the last Space cannot be deleted.
> 8. Correct the Chrome/Brave/Edge import claims now. Build the importer in Phase 2.
> 9. Little windows get no Space commands. "Open in main window" is enough.
> 10. The eleven tests that pin bugs are decided from the spec row and Arc's behaviour. Ask only where those two disagree.
>
> **Checked against the tree, same day.** The profile-migration failure is real, but the folder is created by `PathService` for `DIR_CRASH_DUMPS` (parents included), which Crashpad init requests before migration, not by Crashpad's own `mkdir`. The rail close-button bug is real: a rail row is 44 DIP and the narrow threshold is 42. HTTPS-First balanced mode is registered on and `IsBalancedModeEnabled` ignored that default. macOS still loaded `/Library/Application Support/Google/Chrome/External Extensions`. DuckDuckGo was missing from some regional lists, and the fallback then returned the first engine in the list. The Mac importer lists Safari and Firefox, not Chrome. "No other browser profiles were found" is reachable. The counts in section 1 were not re-run.
>
> **Landed with the patch that carries this note, not yet built.** Migration before Crashpad, and a Crashpad-only folder still migrates. Keychain fails closed and copies a legacy key only for an existing profile. HTTPS-First follows the pref. macOS does not read Chrome's external-extensions directory. DuckDuckGo is the fallback in every region. Rail rows keep the favicon. A plain click does not reset a pin, and the drag ends first. Peek defaults off. Sleep defaults to Never and skips audible, captured, picture-in-picture, fullscreen-video, beforeunload and form tabs, and the on-screen split pane. Clear keeps folders and does not close the last tab. Horizontal switching drops the visibility controller and the folder drag target before the strip view is freed. Move to Space and Route to Space ids are handled on the horizontal strip. A link drop on a folder does not hit `NOTREACHED`. Session writes fetch `SessionService` at each write. The welcome claims in README, INSTALL and W3 match the importer. The cuts, ⌘9's badge, folder-collapse keeping the active tab, delete-Space switching to a neighbour, little-window Space commands, and the rest of Phases 0–6 are not done. Nothing here was compiled.

> **Recheck of 0053–0056, 2026-09-28 (patch 0057).** Patches 0053–0056 claimed this plan done; nothing in them had been built. Patch 0057 finishes the Phase 0 code and proves it on a real build of 155.0.8059.12: `stedding_browser_tests` 103/103, and the Space, jar, restore and archive cases again three times over with six running at once (real windows: clicks on every kind of row, the rail, renames, link drops over folders, Space switches by every path, chip drags in both forms and right to left, Clear from the key and the line, three restarts and restores, the cookie jar of every way a tab opens, Delete browsing data from Settings, the archive sweep and restore, little windows, screenshots, the sleep gate, search defaults, the horizontal strip's tab menu), the unit sweep 807/807, the keychain cases 11/11 and the search cases 8/8 (`components_unittests` parts, built as a local test binary). Ticked below only where the whole "Done when" passed here: TAB-4, TAB-5, TAB-6, TAB-10, TAB-11, TAB-12, TAB-14, TAB-15, TAB-16, TAB-17, TAB-18, SPC-5, SPC-6, SPC-10, SPC-11, SPC-12, SPC-13, CMD-2, CMD-4, CMD-5, CMD-6, CMD-8, WEB-4, WEB-6, WEB-7, PLT-3, REPO-2 (TAB-5, TAB-10, TAB-18, CMD-2, CMD-5 and WEB-6 by browser_tests in place of interactive_ui_tests, which move the real pointer). Everything else in Phase 0 is written and has its test green, and still waits on a part of its "Done when" this machine cannot give: the owner's own steps on the next build (TAB-1, WIN-3, PLT-1, WEB-1, PLT-2), a Windows build (TAB-1, WIN-5, N7), an ASan or dangling-pointer build (TAB-7, TAB-8, SPC-1, SPC-2, WIN-1, WIN-2, WEB-2; none has been made: it needs the disk and hours the owner has not given), VoiceOver (WIN-3), a managed-policy Mac and Mail (CMD-7), Keychain prompts (WEB-1, PLT-2), and a live run of the archive page (WEB-7). Found and fixed on the way: the command bar offered a switch that turned Peek on; the tab menu's Close row said "Close" for any selection; a folder made and collapsed at once kept its rows on screen; coming back to a Space showed an older tab when the last one had arrived active (⌘T, a link opened in a new tab), because the tab was filed after its activation was looked at; the Clear line took presses only where it had last been painted; ⇧⌘K was disabled in private windows; a second quick screenshot was dropped; new tabs made during a Space switch were filed in the Space being left; a page left after keeping its jar was reloaded before its navigation; a settings or new-tab page in a Space with its own session opened the web in the shared jar; the choice program's region predicate was bent instead of the program choice. Open for the owner: whether the Clear line shows in a window with no pinned tabs and in a private window, as CMD-3 and SPC-7 assume; spaces B17 says it does not, and Arc's own behaviour there is to be checked before deciding. 31 upstream search cases still assert a Google default and an EEA-only choice (HANDOFF trap 51). Nothing is released or pushed.

> **Status.** Written 2026-09-26, against Chromium 155.0.8059.12 and the series of 52 patches on it. It replaces an earlier draft of this file that came from an unverified pass.
>
> **Method.** Sixteen review units read every one of the 405 files the series changes (34.5k lines), each twice, and each pass looked through a correctness lens and a product lens. The reports of medium or higher severity were merged by root cause and checked against the code by separate verifiers. Nothing was built or run, so "confirmed" in this plan means traced in the code at HEAD, not reproduced on a machine.

## 1. The verdict: where the project really stands

The beta installs, and on the happy path in a normal Mac window most of it draws what the specs describe. The rest of the code is not ready to ship. Almost every feature Stedding adds on top of Chromium has confirmed defects, and the tests could not have shown them. Nothing was built or run for this audit: every finding is a reading of the code, and each fix is proven on a real build before it closes (section 2).

**What to do next.** Do not release a build of the current series. Start Phase 0 (section 3): first the tools that prove a fix, then the owner's click and rail symptoms, the crashes, the data-loss bugs and the false privacy claims. The owner makes the decisions listed at the end of section 2 before Phase 0 starts.

The worst things, in plain words:

1. **Clicking tabs.** Nothing found so far explains the owner's report that a click on a row does not select it. The plain click path in the open sidebar is upstream code and reads correctly. Two things can break single rows: a rename field that never closes and then swallows clicks, and a favicon reset that leaves a drag running. If every row fails, the cause is somewhere else, such as the window's hit test, and only a test that really clicks will find it. The series has no such test (TAB-1, TST-1).
2. **The collapsed rail is broken by construction.** A hovered rail row puts its close button where the favicon was, so a click there closes the tab. A click on a drifted pin resets it instead of selecting it. The rail's form follows the collapse state instead of the drawn width, and a Space chip moves to the top on any jitter (TAB-2, TAB-3, WIN-3, SPC-6).
3. **Crashes on ordinary gestures.** Found so far: opening a link from a tab inside a folder, dragging a link over a folder, the horizontal-tabs toggle, the Spaces settings page after its window closes, a cached SessionService pointer, actions mode in ⌘T, and a race in the screenshot tool. Folders alone have three use-after-free paths.
4. **Data loss.** Patch 0050's profile migration never runs, because Crashpad creates Stedding's folder first, so every Mac beta user who upgrades to a build with it would open an empty browser. It is not released, and it must not be until PLT-1 is fixed. The keychain fallback in patch 0038 has shipped in every Mac beta since beta 4: when the read of the old "Chromium Safe Storage" item is denied or fails, it makes a new key and the saved passwords and cookies can no longer be decrypted, and a fresh profile adopts Chromium's key. The next release must carry its fix (PLT-1, WEB-1, PLT-2, SER-1).
5. **Commands that destroy more than they say.** Clear can close folders and the whole window. Shift-click and Close Folder's Tabs can close tabs in other Spaces. Sleep discards calls, audio and half-filled forms on a 15-minute default timer. Auto-archive measures idle time wrongly and restores tabs to the wrong Space and cookie jar.
6. **Restore does not keep what the user built.** Nested folders scramble, collapsed folders reopen, tab names are lost, and pin and home changes revert after a relaunch.
7. **Privacy promises that are false.** HTTPS-First is really off. Delete browsing data misses the independent-session jars, tabs start in the wrong jar, and isolation changes reach only one window. Chrome's external-extensions folder is still read on macOS, and DuckDuckGo is not the fallback in 10 regions or for an unknown country, where the default is a random engine, Google included. Copied links can leak tracking parameters. Safe Browsing is probably inert without an API key; that one is plausible, not confirmed (WEB-5 settles it).
8. **Broken core surfaces.** Peek shows no page to anyone and has security holes. The command bar's list, actions mode and ⌘L are broken, and most little-window keys are dead.
9. **The proof is thin.** No Stedding test clicks, hovers or drags a row, and the series has no browser_tests or interactive_ui_tests of its own (confirmed, PLT-F8). By the first-pass tooling and docs audits, which were not re-verified, the sweep also skips tests the specs cite and reports green for checks it never ran.
10. **The docs tell users things that are not true.** Confirmed: README, INSTALL and the welcome spec say the welcome imports from Chrome, Brave and Edge on macOS, and it cannot. By the first-pass docs audit: the pages about contact with Google and the Windows installer for beta 8. The pages call Safe Browsing on and protective, which is unproven until WEB-5 settles it.

**What is proven and what is not.** Every confirmed finding was traced by a second reader to a line of code at HEAD, but none was reproduced. A crash that reads as certain, for example a `NOTREACHED()` that is fatal in M155, is still a reading. The 21 plausible findings have a real mechanism, but whether users can reach them, or what they cause, is not settled. The 260 low findings were not checked. The tests section and the repository section come from the first pass and were not re-verified. The owner's click bug is not explained. It is item one of Phase 0 because nobody knows its cause yet.

**The counts.**

| What was counted | Number |
|---|---|
| Root-cause clusters from the medium-or-higher reports | 359 |
| Refuted by a verifier (they had merged 9 reports) | 6 |
| Standing clusters | 353: 332 confirmed in code, 21 plausible |
| Standing clusters by severity | 24 critical, 91 high, 210 medium, 28 low |
| Low-severity reports, listed but not verified | 260 |
| Tooling findings (platform section, `PLT-T` rows) | 25 |
| Stedding tests that would fail if their behaviour broke (first pass) | 85 of 265, all unit tests |

| Work items | 64 P0, 87 P1, 101 P2, 44 P3 |

## 2. Ground rules from now on

**The definition of done.** A green unit test does not close anything. An item is closed only when all of these hold:

1. Its "Done when" check passes on a real build: the shipping config of the current pin, launched as the app and driven through the UI. Record the date, the commit and the result next to the item's checkbox.
2. A test that goes through the user's path, not a helper, was seen red before the fix and green after, and a `tooling/dev test` filter runs it. Input, focus, drag and restore need interactive_ui_tests or browser_tests with `PRE_` steps. Unit tests are fine for pure logic, but they never count as the proof for a spec row.
3. The spec row cites that test, and its comments and spec text say what the code now does.
4. Suites that touch lifetimes run clean under ASan or with dangling-pointer checks on.

Captures prove pixels only. The old rule, "a behaviour is shipped when its test is green", is withdrawn.

**One item at a time.** Finish an item's "Done when" before starting the next. Items that share a root cause (the table in section 3) are one change. Fixups go into the patch that introduced the code, following the series workflow in `docs/HANDOFF.md`. SER-2's tools check each one.

**No new features until Phase 0 and Phase 1 are done.** New operator feedback goes into this plan as an item in the right phase. It is built at once only if it is a crash, data loss or a broken privacy promise.

**Confirm plausible findings first.** Items and findings marked plausible, or whose text says "confirm first", are reproduced before anyone fixes them: a failing test, or the failure seen in a real build. If an honest attempt does not reproduce one, record the attempt and close it as not reproduced. Nothing is fixed blind.

**Low findings are leads.** Before editing a file, read its low rows in section 4. Check each one, and fix it in the same change when it is true and small.

**First-pass sections are claims.** Check the tests section, the repository section and the `PLT-T` tooling rows against the tree before acting on them.

**Input-driving tests need a free machine.** interactive_ui_tests move the real pointer and keyboard. Run them only when nobody is using the machine, or on a dedicated machine or virtual display. The rule "never inject input while someone is at the machine" still holds. PLT-3 keeps the capture tools off the owner's own browser.

**PLAN.md and BACKLOG.md.** PLAN.md sets the order of work until Phase 1 is done, and it supersedes BACKLOG.md's order until then. BACKLOG keeps its ids. REPO-1 is narrowed accordingly: add the two missing top ids (tabs that do not select on click, and the crashes) and a line pointing here. Point AGENTS.md's "Current priorities" and HANDOFF's "Open items" here too. When Phase 1 is done, rebuild BACKLOG.md from what is left: every open item here becomes an S- id, or maps to one. After that PLAN.md carries only Phases 2 to 6, and it is deleted when it is empty.

**Decisions the owner makes before Phase 0 starts.** Each one is recommended in section 4. None is applied without a yes.

- Remove horizontal tab mode from Stedding windows (WIN-1, SPC-3, CMD-2).
- Ship Peek off until its host is rebuilt (CMD-4).
- Default the Space sleep timer to Never until sleep is gated (TAB-14).
- Approve the cuts in these items:
  - Tabs: drop-between-rows inside a folder (folders F10); folders moving as whole units; the plain-click favicon reset (H5); the ⌘T actions the specs name but that do not exist; the greyscale sleeping favicon; the Android and tab-storage FOLDER hunks.
  - Spaces: the throwaway Space, Copy Tab to Space, the clipboard fence, HostRouteFor, the stedding.split restore key, the orphan embedder SpaceModel, and Blank Window in-memory isolation (S18).
  - Commands: actions mode's Chromium registry rows, the Mac Tab menu's group rows, and, unless the owner answers the little-window question below with SPC-26, ⌃1–9 and the "Open in <Space>" rows in little windows.
  - Settings and import: scheduled sidebar backups and backup restore, the little-window idle close and popup archiver, the `stedding.sidebar` v2 module, the automatic bookmarks-to-pins conversion after an import, and the little-window toggle on Windows.
  - Tooling: capture hooks in official builds, check-geometry as a CI check, `capture-ui`, and the separate window lookups in the capture tools. windows N8 goes back to partial.
- Answer the open product questions: what ⌘9 selects (REPO-5, CMD-25); what collapsing the active tab's folder does (TAB-11); whether Delete on the active Space switches to a neighbour first or is disabled (SPC-20, WEB-20); whether to build the Chromium-family importer (WEB-15) or withdraw the claim for good (WEB-14); whether little windows get Space commands (SPC-26) or lose them (CMD-15); and the intended behaviour behind the eleven tests that pin bugs (TST-12).

Items in several areas share a root cause. Do each group as one change and close all of its ids together.

### Phase 0: stop the line

**Goal.** No known crash, no data loss, no broken privacy promise, and a way to prove each fix. Also the owner's three symptoms (clicks, crashes, the rail) either explained and fixed, or reproduced by a test.

**0.1 Tools to prove things.** These come first, because every later item is closed by them.
- PLT-4: add browser_tests and interactive_ui_tests targets. It is P1, pulled forward because TAB-1 and TST-1 need it.
- REPO-2, with PLT-12 (P2, same change): the sweeps run what the docs cite, and a filter that matches nothing fails.
- SER-2: the series safety tools, before more fixups land. P1, pulled forward.
- PLT-7: guard the commands that rewrite the checkout, and make the build lock hold. P1, pulled forward.
- PLT-3: one PID-based window helper, so the capture and drive tools never touch the owner's browser.
- REPO-1, as narrowed in section 2: the top ids in BACKLOG and the pointers to this plan.
- TST-1 to TST-5, written first and seen red: a real click, the rail, every way to switch Space, restart and restore, and inserts next to folder tabs.

**0.2 The owner's symptoms.**
- TAB-1: find why a click does not select, with TST-1.
- TAB-2: define a rail row once, so no close button replaces the favicon.
- TAB-3: rewrite OnMouseReleased so the drag always ends and a rail click never resets a pin.
- TAB-4, with SPC-29 (P1, same change): end every rename on focus loss.
- WIN-3: choose the rail form from the drawn width, and fix the click-handler leak.
- SPC-6: chip reordering in the rail that knows its orientation and has a threshold.
- SPC-5: the visibility pass honours folder collapse.

**0.3 Crashes and use-after-free.**
- WIN-1, SPC-3, CMD-2 and TAB-18: remove horizontal tab mode and range-check the menu ids that remain.
- SPC-1 and WEB-2: the settings handler observes the registry, not a window's model.
- TAB-7 and SPC-2: no cached SessionService pointers.
- TAB-8: FolderView subscribes to node destruction.
- TAB-9: the drop on a folder header, rebuilt without detaching.
- SPC-4: SpaceDragTarget rewritten to stay attached.
- TAB-5: link drops that know about FOLDER.
- TAB-6: folder interiors addressable for inserts, moves, splits and Move Out.
- CMD-1: actions mode uses a Stedding allow-list instead of Chromium's registry.
- WIN-2: screenshot lifetimes without raw pointers across async steps.

**0.4 Data loss and destructive commands.**
- PLT-1: rewrite the macOS profile migration. It blocks any release.
- WEB-1, PLT-2 and SER-1, after PLT-1: the keychain fails closed and copies a legacy key once. The fail-closed read and the copy go into 0038 as one fixup. The gate on migrated profiles reads PLT-1's marker, which patch 0050 does not have today, so it lands with PLT-1 in 0050 rather than as a forward reference from 0038.
- SPC-7 and CMD-3: Clear keeps folders and never closes the window.
- TAB-10: range selection stays inside the visible rows.
- TAB-13: Close Folder's Tabs acts on the active Space only.
- TAB-11, then TAB-12: folder title and collapse go through the model, and persistence is one pass in tree order.
- TAB-14: gate every sleep, with the timer at Never until then.
- TAB-15 and SPC-8: handle contents replacement and re-write the session data.
- SPC-9: jar rebinds do not destroy pages.
- WEB-6: the archive sweep skips tabs in use and never activates anything.
- WEB-7, with SPC-18 (P1, same change): closed entries carry Space, folder and pin, and restore by id.

**0.5 Privacy and security promises.**
- TAB-16: HTTPS-First balanced mode really on.
- TAB-17: pin homes accept http and https only.
- WEB-3: no reading of Chrome's External Extensions folder.
- WEB-4: DuckDuckGo as the fallback in every region.
- WEB-5: settle whether Safe Browsing works without a key.
- WIN-4: decide "private" in one place.
- WIN-5: copies leak no tracking data and no private-window content.
- SPC-10: upstream garbage collection keeps Space jars.
- SPC-11: Delete browsing data reaches every jar.
- SPC-12 and CMD-8: choose the jar before contents exist.
- SPC-13, with WEB-21 (P1, same change): isolation changes apply in every window, and the switch tells the truth.
- CMD-7: little windows open through upstream's safe-profile handling.

**0.6 Broken core surfaces: ship them off, or rewrite them.**
- CMD-4: Peek off by default. This lands first.
- CMD-9 and CMD-10: rebuild the peek host, and peek only on a user gesture. Once CMD-4 has landed, they block only turning Peek back on, so the owner may move them to Phase 2.
- CMD-5: the command bar's list layer rewritten around one Row vector.
- CMD-6: the bar lists only the active Space while Hide Other Spaces is on.

**0.7 Stop telling users false things.** These are doc edits that take minutes.
- WEB-14 (P1, pulled forward): correct the import claims in README, INSTALL and welcome W3.
- REPO-3's interim step: until the record exists, no page calls Safe Browsing "on by default" or a "protection". The rest of REPO-3 is in Phase 5.

**Exit criteria.**
- TST-1 to TST-5 are green in `tooling/dev test`, and each was seen red.
- Every P0 item's "Done when" passes on a real build, and the result is recorded.
- The Phase 0 suites report nothing under ASan or dangling-pointer checks.
- The click symptom is either reproduced by TST-1 and fixed, or the owner's exact steps are a TST-1 case that passes, and the owner confirms it on their machine.
- The approved cuts are applied, and their spec rows are withdrawn.

### Phase 1: the core loop

**Goal.** What a user does a hundred times an hour works every time: click and select rows, use the rail and folders, switch Spaces, quit and relaunch.

**Tabs, folders and the sidebar.**
- TAB-19, with SPC-19: one owner of row visibility, and folders that belong to a Space.
- TAB-20: folder rows laid out in model order.
- TAB-21: the folder commands (nest in place, no essentials, peel emptied chains, reentrancy).
- TAB-22: folder rows in the sidebar's colours.
- TAB-23: the rail's look (favicon size, Clear line, combo slot, essentials grid).
- TAB-24, with SPC-34 (P2, same change): each Space's pins stay above the Clear line.
- TAB-25, with CMD-24 (P2, same change): one rule for closing and sleeping pins and essentials.
- TAB-26: a pin's home comes from its first committed URL.

**Spaces and switching.**
- SPC-16: a switch lands on the tab the user left.
- SPC-15, with CMD-14 (same change): essentials follow the user, and ⌘T's go-to-tab keeps the current Space for an essential.
- SPC-17: the Space follows an activation from outside Stedding.
- SPC-28: the switcher stops changing under the pointer.
- SPC-27, with WIN-20 (P2, same change): the Space slide as a real two-list slide, and the owner's call on whether the swipe tracks the finger.
- SPC-21: Spaces deleted or added in another window.
- SPC-22: new windows open on the source Space.
- SPC-20, with WEB-20: an honest Delete Space, including from settings.
- SPC-24: a deleted Space's jar is wiped once, after its tabs leave.
- SPC-30: guard the posted jar rebind.

**Restore.**
- SPC-14: consume restore data once, and delete stedding.split.
- SPC-25: seed a restored window's Spaces by id.
- SPC-23: session cookies and sessionStorage survive a restart in Space jars.

**Proof** (P1 test items, pulled forward from Phase 3 because they prove this phase):
- TST-6: the core-loop acceptance script. It is this phase's exit gate.
- TST-7: drags onto a folder header and a Space chip.
- TST-8: a selection never spans Spaces or collapsed folders.
- TST-9: the core keyboard, with real key events.

**Exit criteria.**
- TST-6 passes on an installed build, and the run is recorded.
- TST-7 to TST-9 are green.
- The owner uses the build as their daily browser, with their own profile, for at least a day. Any failure in the core loop becomes an item and blocks the exit.
- BACKLOG.md is rebuilt as section 2 describes.

**Release checkpoint.** Users are on beta 8 (M153), behind stable. Recommendation: once Phase 1 exits, cut a signed, notarized beta with Phase 6's steps, after the user-facing doc items that gate a release (Phase 5). Do not wait for Phases 2 to 4.

### Phase 2: the rest of the features, finish or cut, area by area

**Goal.** Every remaining feature either meets its spec or is cut, with its rows withdrawn. Work one area at a time, P1 items first, in the order below.

**2.1 Window, toolbar and page.**
- WIN-6, with SPC-47: AddressRow and the page colour owned per window, private windows included.
- WIN-7: AddressRow as a passive rule.
- WIN-8: paint the exact page colour.
- WIN-9: the address row in immersive full screen. Confirm first.
- WIN-10: the empty address row drags the window.
- WIN-11, with TAB-30, SPC-41, CMD-25 and REPO-5's ⌘9 part: badges hide on deactivation, and one list decides ⌘1–9 as the owner chose.
- WIN-12, with TAB-31 and SPC-44: the Space swipe on Windows and Linux.
- WIN-13: copy only the visible selection.
- WIN-14: screenshot codecs off the UI thread.
- WIN-15, with WEB-11: bookmarks-to-pins as a one-time, reversible default.
- Then WIN-16 to WIN-28, less WIN-17 (Phase 3), WIN-20 (Phase 1, with SPC-27), WIN-25 and WIN-26 (Phase 4) and WIN-28 (Phase 5, with REPO-11).

**2.2 The command bar, keys, menus, peek and little windows.**
- CMD-11: a navigate-this-tab mode for ⌘L.
- CMD-12: focus comes back when the bar closes.
- CMD-13: a ranked, internationalised matcher.
- CMD-15 and SPC-26: mark little windows at creation. The owner decides which Space commands they keep.
- CMD-16: the new-tab peek hook in a non-views target.
- CMD-17: the peek follows its window.
- CMD-18: Chromium's commands in windows without Spaces.
- CMD-19: Mac pane focus on F6. Confirm first.
- Then CMD-20 to CMD-41, less CMD-24 (Phase 1, with TAB-25), CMD-25 (2.1, with WIN-11), CMD-32 and CMD-33 (Phase 3) and CMD-38 (Phase 4). If CMD-9 and CMD-10 were deferred, they come before Peek is turned back on.

**2.3 Settings, welcome, archive and import.**
- WEB-8: the archiver's idle clock.
- WEB-9: Clear archive acts only on the rows shown.
- WEB-10: the archive opens in the active Space.
- WEB-11: done with WIN-15.
- WEB-12: the import dialog and profile editor, with People hidden.
- WEB-13: the welcome's search step as the only first-run chooser.
- WEB-15: the Chromium-family importer, if the owner chooses to build it.
- WEB-16: harden the sidebar parser.
- WEB-17: ⌘T Import from Arc opens the summary.
- WEB-18: cut the backups.
- WEB-19: de-duplicate Import sidebar by durable identity.
- Then WEB-22 to WEB-46, less WEB-24 (2.5, with SPC-36), WEB-36 to WEB-39 (Phase 3) and WEB-43 (2.5, with TAB-35). WEB-20 and WEB-21 are in earlier phases.

**2.4 Platform and tooling.**
- PLT-5: macOS native-messaging hosts.
- SER-3: the three hard-coded org.chromium.Chromium sites follow the bundle id.
- PLT-6: pin tooling that cannot downgrade.
- PLT-8: tooling under bash 3.2, and a macOS CI job.
- Then PLT-14 and PLT-16 to PLT-19. PLT-12 is in Phase 0 with REPO-2, PLT-13 in Phase 3, PLT-15 in Phase 4, PLT-9 in Phase 5 with REPO-13, and PLT-10 and PLT-11 in Phase 6.

**2.5 Tabs and Spaces, P2 and P3.** TAB-27 to TAB-43 and SPC-31 to SPC-57, less the items placed elsewhere: TAB-30, TAB-31, SPC-41, SPC-44 and SPC-47 (2.1), SPC-34 (Phase 1, with TAB-24), TAB-37, TAB-38 and SPC-48 to SPC-50 (Phase 3), and TAB-41, SPC-51 and SPC-53 (Phase 4). Do TAB-33 with SPC-31, SPC-36 with WEB-24, and TAB-35 with WEB-43.

**Exit criteria.** Every spec row is finished, meaning its "Done when" passes, or it is withdrawn. No P1 item placed in Phases 0 to 2 is open. Each P2 or P3 item placed in Phase 2 is done, or moved to BACKLOG with a reason.

### Phase 3: tests and claims reset

**Goal.** The tests prove the product, and the specs claim only what is proven. A feature's tests are written with its fix in Phases 0 to 2, red first. This phase covers what is left and resets the claims.

- TST-10: the command bar through its real entry points. Write it with CMD-5 and CMD-11 if they land first.
- TST-11: delete the cut list of tests.
- TST-12: invert the eleven tests that pin bugs, after the owner confirms each intended behaviour.
- TST-13, with SPC-50: re-mark every spec row, and make `check-repo` fail on a cited test that does not exist.
- TST-14: the upstream suites the series turned red. Its search-engine suites are done with WEB-4 in Phase 0.
- Then TST-15 to TST-25, less TST-23 (Phase 6, with PLT-11). Also the area test and spec items: TAB-37, TAB-38, SPC-48, SPC-49, WIN-17, CMD-32, CMD-33, WEB-36 to WEB-39 and PLT-13.

Some of these items conflict with the cuts, so adjust them if the cuts are approved:
- TST-16 tests little-window ⌃1–9 and the idle close.
- TST-19 tests backup restore.
- TST-12 lists ⌃1–9 in little windows and throwaway persistence.
- TST-3's horizontal-mode case becomes "the toggle cannot be reached".

**Exit criteria.**
- TST-13's check passes.
- No row reads "verified" without a product-path test and a recorded acceptance run.
- No weak test is the only citation of a row.
- The release sweep runs green every upstream suite the series touches.

### Phase 4: the patch series re-cut

**Goal.** A smaller upstream footprint. Every patch builds on its own, and the tree stays identical. SER-4 says to begin only after the P0 and P1 bug work has shipped, so this phase starts after a release that carries Phases 0 and 1, with no fixup in flight and no pin move under way.

- SER-4: re-cut Phase A to 18 patches with an identical tree.
- SER-5: revert the hunks that only reformat.
- SER-6: defaults through the T02 hooks.
- SER-7: settings hooks, with the strings in the grdp.
- SER-8: commands, keys and menus through hooks.
- SER-9: SidebarController and TabRowDecorator.
- SER-10: folder operations out of TabStripModel.
- SER-11: the platform files.
- SER-12: wire or withdraw every dead function (S-61).
- SER-13: capture scaffolding out of product code.
- SER-14: CI enforces the boundary rules.
- SER-15: Stedding-owned source roots.
- SER-16: branding as a build-time override.
- SER-17: send the signer fixes upstream.
- The area items that shrink the upstream surface: TAB-41, SPC-51, SPC-53, WIN-25, WIN-26, CMD-38 and PLT-15.

**Exit criteria.** SER-4's check passes: `git diff --quiet OLD HEAD`, `HEAD^{tree}` equals `patches/series.tree`, and every layer boundary builds. CI enforces B1 to B7 on every change to `patches/`.

### Phase 5: repository and docs cleanup

**Goal.** Every page a person reads is true, and the internal docs tell the next person what is true. The user-facing items (REPO-3, REPO-4, REPO-7, REPO-8, REPO-9, REPO-10 and REPO-11), and REPO-13 with PLT-9, which decides what gets built, gate any release, including the Phase 1 checkpoint.

- REPO-3, after WEB-5: state the facts on Safe Browsing and contact with Google.
- REPO-4: PRIVACY.md describes the shipped build only.
- REPO-5: generate SHORTCUTS.md. The ⌘9 decision was made before Phase 0 and built with WIN-11 in Phase 2.
- REPO-6: a build guide people can follow.
- REPO-7: INSTALL, SECURITY and CONTRIBUTING true, without duplication.
- REPO-8: rewrite README.md.
- REPO-9: FAQ.md as the only FAQ. If WEB-18 is approved, drop its wording about restoring sidebar backups.
- REPO-10: errata and an index for the release notes. Editing the public GitHub release pages needs the owner's approval.
- REPO-11: VERSION moves with the pin, with WIN-28.
- REPO-12: public-repo hygiene.
- REPO-13, with PLT-9: decide which build config ships, record it in an ADR, and make packaging and publishing enforce it.
- Then REPO-14 to REPO-20.

**Exit criteria.** Every item's "Done when" passes, and the `check-repo` checks they add run in CI.

### Phase 6: the release

**Goal.** A signed, notarized macOS release of the current pin, then Windows.

**macOS.**
- Build the image in the shipping config that PLT-9 and REPO-13 recorded in Phase 5, and write its provenance file. Check that VERSION matches the build (REPO-11, done in Phase 5).
- Sign and notarize the image with the signer that has worked since 2026-09-23 (S-17).
- Run PLT-1's migration on a real beta-8 profile. Then run TST-6 and the TST-22 scripts on the installed image, and record the results.
- Write the notes with the codec and stable-lag lines (REPO-10).
- The owner approves publishing. It is a public action.
- After the release: the updater behind its settings entry (ADR 0014).

**Windows.**
- PLT-10: the elevator and tracing IIDs, and real generated GUIDs.
- PLT-11, with TST-23 (same change): the identity tests assert Stedding's values, and the Windows sweep runs them.
- S-56: installer, signing, updates and CI.

**Exit criteria.**
- The owner has published a signed, notarized DMG.
- The release notes are true.
- The acceptance runs are recorded.
- The Windows installer ships for the same tag, or the notes say it is not included.

## 4. Area by area

The next six sections are new and built from verified findings, one per code area; the one exception is the platform section's `PLT-T` tooling rows, which come from the first pass. In each, "Work, in order" lists the items with their fixes and "Done when" checks, "Findings" lists the verified clusters with their severity and state, and "Low-severity findings (not verified)" lists leads. The last two sections, on tests and on the repository, come from the first pass and were not re-verified. There is a note where they begin.

### Tabs, folders and the sidebar

This area is where most of what the owner sees goes wrong, and the audit found concrete causes for most of it. Nothing was run: every finding below was traced in the code at HEAD, and "confirmed" means traced, not reproduced. The collapsed rail is broken by construction. Patch 0039 made rail rows 44 DIP wide, just above the 42 DIP "narrow" threshold, so a hovered rail row puts its close button where the favicon was, and a click there closes the tab instead of selecting it. A plain click on a drifted pin's icon navigates the pin home instead of switching to it. That reset path also leaves a drag controller running, and while it runs the window cannot be closed. In the open sidebar the plain click path is upstream code and selects correctly on paper. What can break single rows is a rename field that never closes after a double-click and then swallows clicks. If every row fails, the cause lies outside this code (the window's hit test) and needs a test that really clicks; the series has none. Folders are the weakest part. They were added as a new collection type without teaching the upstream index, drop and drag code about them. The result is crashes on ordinary gestures: opening a link from a folder tab, dragging a link over a folder, moving a nested folder's tabs out. There are two use-after-free paths in the folder views and one in folder persistence. Restore scrambles nested folders, collapsed folders pop open again, folder headers show in every Space, and rows inside a folder never move on screen. Sleeping discards tabs with no checks. The default 15-minute Space timer kills calls, audio and unsaved input, drops custom tab names and may take an Independent-session tab out of its own cookie jar. Shift-click selection takes in tabs of other Spaces, so Cmd-W can close tabs the user never saw. HTTPS-First "on by default" is actually off, while the Stedding row reads on. The tests exercise helpers, constants and states the product never creates, so all of this ships with green runs. 69 verified findings (6 critical, 22 high, 37 medium, 4 low) and 74 unverified lows.

#### Features

| Feature | Real state | Why (finding ids) | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| Click a row to select it (open sidebar and rail) | Rail: broken. A hovered row shows a close button in place of its favicon, and a click on a drifted pin resets it. Open sidebar: no cause found in this code. A stuck rename field or a leaked drag controller breaks single rows | TAB-F2, F15, F25, F14, F26, F16, F58, F27 | Fix (TAB-1 to TAB-4) |
| Collapsed rail look (sidebar Y6–Y9, Y12; folders F13) | Partly. Rows are 44 DIP but favicons paint at 16 DIP; the Clear label is clipped; there may be an empty slot under the toggle; the hover-expanded rail keeps rail-shaped essentials | TAB-F2, F37, F33, F63, L5 | Fix |
| Close glyph on hover (tabs R1–R3) | Works in the open sidebar, broken in the rail. The hover X and middle-click destroy a Space pin that Cmd-W would only sleep | TAB-F2, F59 | Fix |
| Rename in place (tabs R14, folders F4) | Partly. Enter and Escape work. The field never closes on blur and then swallows clicks; Enter on the untouched text freezes the page title; a folder toggles before it renames; the Cmd-T action the spec names does not exist | TAB-F14, F26, F60, F31, L1 | Fix; withdraw the Cmd-T claim |

Fixes: TAB-F2, TAB-L16. With `IsRailRow` made false again (the old rule), both fail.

On open rows, reset only through a visible hover reset glyph like the card badge, and update spec H5 to say so. Fixes: TAB-F15, TAB-F25, TAB-F16, TAB-F58, TAB-L15, TAB-L71. - [x] **TAB-14** (P0) Gate every sleep, and default the Space sleep timer to Never until the gate lands. Done when: in Space A play audio in one tab, join a call or share a screen in a second, pop a video into picture-in-picture in a third and type into a form in a fourth; switch to Space B and wait past a 5-minute timer: chrome://discards shows none of the four discarded; Sleep Other Tabs with a split on screen leaves both panes loaded; a browser_test runs the timer through the real lifecycle unit and asserts WasDiscarded is false for those tabs and true for a plain one. **Proven 2026-09-28, patch 0057:** `SleepInUseTest.ALeftSpaceKeepsItsTabsInUseAwake` runs the Space timer, on a 200 ms delay, through the real lifecycle unit: the idle tab sleeps, and a tab playing sound, one in a call (a microphone stream held by the capture indicator, which the gate reads), one in picture-in-picture and one with typed input stay awake; `SleepInUseTest.SleepOthersKeepsBothPanesOnScreen`. #### Findings

| ID | Sev | State | Where (path:line, patch) | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TAB-F1 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/views/tabs/common/tab_link_drop_handler.cc:38-50; 0008 | A link, text or file dragged over a folder (or above the list when a folder is first) reaches `default: NOTREACHED()` in GetDropIndexForNode, which is fatal in M155. | Handle FOLDER in the drop handler and recurse into an expanded FolderView in GetLinkDropIndex. |
| TAB-F2 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/views/tabs/common/tab_view_vertical_layout.cc:75-90; 0039, 0017 | Rail rows are 44 DIP, above the 42 DIP "narrow" threshold, so a hovered rail row lays out the close button in the centre and hides the favicon; a click closes the tab. | One IsRailRow helper used by RowState, Layout and the drift dot. |
| TAB-F3 | critical | confirmed | components/tabs/impl/tab_collection.cc:320; components/tabs/impl/unpinned_tab_collection.cc:18; 0008 | Inserting or moving at an index inside a folder hits a CHECK in ToDirectIndex or FindMovePositionRecursive, e.g. a link opened from any folder tab but the last. | Resolve folder interiors in GetInsertionDetails and GetMovePosition. |
| TAB-F4 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/tabs/stedding_folder_session.cc:106; 0008 (SpaceModel copy: 0004) | FolderSessionService caches a raw SessionService* that ShutdownForProfile destroys while the window can live on ("Close all windows", then Stay). | Fetch the service with GetForProfileIfExisting at each write, or observe OnDestroying. |
| TAB-F5 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/views/tabs/common/tab_link_drop_handler.cc:49; 0008 | Same NOTREACHED as TAB-F1, reported by the model review: FOLDER has no case in the link-drop switch. | Handle FOLDER there; audit every `default: NOTREACHED()` over the node type. |
| TAB-F6 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/stedding/sleep_tabs.cc:122-136; 0019, 0020 | Every sleep path calls DiscardTab(EXTERNAL) with no eligibility check; the default 15-minute Space timer discards audible, capturing, PiP and form tabs and skips beforeunload. | One eligibility helper for every sleep path; the timer uses performance_manager's policy. |
| TAB-F7 | high | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/views/tabs/common/folder_view.h:100; 0008 | FolderView keeps a raw node pointer with no will-destroy subscription; the node is freed before the view during the removal animation, and theme, drag and menu paths read it. | Subscribe with RegisterWillDestroyCallback, null the node and guard every use. |
| TAB-F8 | high | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/views/tabs/common/folder_drag_target.h:76; 0008 | hovered_view_ is an untracked raw FolderView*; dropping a nested folder's only tab on its own header deletes the view and then dereferences it. | Use a views::ViewTracker or resolve from the folder handle; clear the highlight before mutating. |

### Spaces

Spaces are the spine of the product, and the core mechanism is sound. There is one tab strip filtered by a hidden-tab predicate, membership is decided when a tab is inserted, deleting a Space moves its tabs instead of closing them, and a profile-wide SpaceRegistry is mirrored by every window. Around that core, the audit verified 97 problems (10 critical, 28 high, 56 medium, 3 low; 7 of them plausible rather than confirmed) and left 76 lows unverified. Nothing was run, so "confirmed" means traced in the code, not reproduced.

Four crash paths start in this area:
- The settings page's Spaces handler outlives the window whose model it observes.
- SpaceModel caches a SessionService that Close-profile deletes.
- The row-visibility controller outlives the tab strip view once the View menu switches to horizontal tabs.
- Dropping a tab-group header on a Space chip hits an upstream CHECK.

Part of "the rail looks weird" comes from here too. Collapsed folders spring open on every switch or new tab. Folder headers and split rows from other Spaces stay in the list. Any drag or jittery click on a rail chip moves that Space to the top in every window. The switcher changes form at the start of the collapse animation.

Clear closes tabs inside folders, including folders created by the importers, and can close the whole window.

Independent sessions (per-Space cookie jars) does not keep its privacy promise:
- Delete browsing data never reaches a Space's jar.
- New, routed, new-window and reopened tabs start in the wrong jar.
- Isolation changes apply only to the window where they were made.
- Every sleep or jar rebind drops the tab's Space from the session log.

Per-tab state (Space, Space pin, home URL) lives in per-window maps. Those maps are cleared when a tab is detached and refilled from restore data that is never consumed. The result is that after a relaunch, unpinning and "Make This the Pinned Page" do not stick.

The reviewers found nothing in this area that stops a click from selecting a tab row. That symptom belongs to the tab-view and drag units. The Space slide (patch 0052) does run on every switch path, but all it does is fade the new rows in with a 36 DIP nudge. Many spec rows marked "built" rest on tests that drive helpers or a model without a registry, and about a dozen helpers ship with green tests and no product caller.

Closing a Space's last tab briefly activates another Space's tab. | SPC-F14, SPC-F34, SPC-F45, SPC-F46, SPC-F48, SPC-F53 | Fix |

| Essentials (B6) | Partly works. Unpinning sends the tab back to the Space it was first filed in, which hides it. Choosing an essential in ⌘T switches Space. A Space pin survives the tab becoming an essential. | SPC-F19, SPC-F51 | Fix |
| Space pins and home URL (B10, B17, B21; pins H1, H2, H6) | Broken for any tab restored from a session: unpin and Make This the Pinned Page are reverted. Pins are also lost on detach or a chip drop. The pinned run is enforced only at pin time. | SPC-F18, SPC-F30, SPC-F47, SPC-F52 | Fix |

Only the first tab of a selection moves. Pin, home, folder and position are lost, and a false routing toast appears. | SPC-F9, SPC-F33, SPC-F47, SPC-F65 | Fix: rewrite on FolderDragTarget's model and change membership in place |
| Space slide (B32) | Partly works. It runs on every SetActiveSpace path but is only a fade-in of the new rows. It is skipped for registry-driven switches and is untested. | SPC-F74, SPC-F75 | Fix |

| Ctrl-Tab recent-tabs strip (switcher X1-X3) | Partly works. The keyboard path works. Tab > Select Next Tab leaves the strip stuck, the strip goes stale, and it is not accessible. | SPC-F86 | Fix |

Isolation is a no-op in it, yet S18 is marked built. | SPC-F39, SPC-F40, SPC-F58, SPC-F59, SPC-F72 | Simplify: persist blankness, withdraw S18, and hide the isolation surfaces in Blank Windows |

#### Work, in order

- [ ] **SPC-1** (P0) Stop the Spaces settings handler from observing a per-window SpaceModel: observe the profile's SpaceRegistry, resolve the model per message, and give SpaceModel::Observer an OnSpaceModelDestroying fired from ~SpaceModel with a check_empty observer list. Fixes: SPC-F1, SPC-F7. Done when: an interactive_ui_test (and a person) opens chrome://settings/stedding in window A, drags the tab into window B, closes A, then reloads and closes the settings tab under ASan without a crash, and the page shows B's Spaces throughout.
- [ ] **SPC-2** (P0) Remove the cached SessionService pointer from SpaceModel and FolderSessionService, and resolve SessionServiceFactory::GetForProfileIfExisting at each write. Fixes: SPC-F2, SPC-F12, SPC-F32. Done when: a browser_test opens a page with a beforeunload handler, runs profiles::CloseProfileWindows, answers Stay, then switches Space, presses ⌘D and moves a tab to another Space, all without a crash under ASan. **Status 2026-09-28, patch 0057:** `DefaultsTest.StayingAfterCloseAllKeepsEverythingWorking` runs these steps (Stay, a Space switch, ⌘D, a move to another Space) in a normal build; the ASan run is open.
- [ ] **SPC-3** (P0) Hide IDC_TOGGLE_VERTICAL_TABS in Stedding windows. Reset space_tab_visibility_ and folder_drag_target_ in VerticalTabStripRegionView::OnTabStripViewWillClear, and hold the controller's root through a views::ViewTracker so the controller does nothing once the view is gone. Fixes: SPC-F8, SPC-F10. Done when: neither the Mac View menu nor the app menu offers the toggle, and an interactive_ui_test that calls ToggleVerticalTabs twice, then presses ⌘T and ⌃2, runs clean under ASan.

No window tears off and nothing crashes. The plain, pinned and selected tabs arrive in that Space with their pin, home, folder and position. The essential and the group header are refused.

A views test asserts the folder's TabViews stay hidden after the posted pass. **Proven 2026-09-28, patch 0057,** and a bug found on the way: a folder made and collapsed at once kept its rows, because the rows followed a collapse only once a pass had reached the folder's view; the controller now hears the collection tree itself. `FolderTabsTest.ACollapsedFolderStaysCollapsed` checks the drawn rows after each step, a posted pass included.

  - Show the dragged chip and the insertion gap.

A view test covers both forms. **Proven 2026-09-28, patch 0057:** `SpaceSwitchTest.AShakyClickOnAChipMovesNothing` (the rail), `.AChipDraggedAlongTheRowMovesItsSpace` and `.AChipDraggedInTheRailMovesItsSpace` (third chip second, in a window already open and a new one), `.ADraggedChipShowsWhereItLands`, `.ADragThatLosesThePointerMovesNothing`, `SpaceSwitchRtlTest.MoveLeftMovesTheChipLeft` and `.AChipDraggedInTheRowMovesItsSpace` (a right-to-left UI), all in a real window.

The restore side is `RestoreTest.TheSidebarComesBack` (a named tab asleep, a napping pin, an isolated Space's jar). After the fix, with a half-typed form in an isolated Space, pinning that tab, moving it to a shared Space and toggling isolation each prompt before the page is lost. An extension page keeps its localStorage across Spaces. **Status 2026-09-29, patch 0057:** everything but the person's own run passes as browser_tests. The confirm-first step: `SpaceJarTest.TheIsolationQuestionCountsOpenPages` and the two below show chrome://settings and an extension page on the profile's jar in a Space with its own session. A half-typed page keeps its jar until it is left, rather than asking, for all three changes: pinning (`AnEssentialWithChangesKeepsItsJarUntilLeft`), a move to a shared Space (`APageWithChangesKeepsItsJarUntilLeft`) and turning isolation off (`TurningIsolationOffKeepsAPageWithChanges`); a form it sends goes in its session (`AFormFromAPageWithChangesUsesItsSession`); the question asked before an isolation change counts the pages it reloads (`TheIsolationQuestionCountsOpenPages`; the dialog itself has no test). `SpaceJarTest.IsolationFromSettingsKeepsTheSettingsPage` turns a Space's session on through the settings page's own message and the page is neither replaced nor reloaded while the Space's web page moves; `SpaceJarTest.AnExtensionPageKeepsItsStorageInEverySpace` reads in a Space with its own session what the extension's page stored in a shared one, and a move leaves it as it is (with `PageBelongsInSpaceJar` answering yes for them, both fail).   - SyncWithRegistry remembers each entry's (isolated, profile_id, in_memory) and rebinds the Spaces whose jar changed.
  - SetSpaceIsolated stops rebinding directly.
  - The registry fires OnSpaceJarWiped, and every window answers by reloading its tabs of that Space.

  Fixes: SPC-F17, SPC-F20, SPC-F27, SPC-F28. Done when: with Work open in two windows, turning Independent session on in window 1 moves Work's tabs in window 2 into the jar too (a browser_test checks both windows' partitions), turning it off moves both back, and Clear Independent Session reloads Work's tabs in both. **Proven 2026-09-28, patch 0057:** `SpaceJarTest.IsolationChangesReachEveryWindow` (both windows' partitions, off and on again), `SpaceJarTest.ClearingTheSessionReloadsEveryWindow` (Work's tabs in both windows reload signed out).

#### Findings

| ID | Sev | State | Where (path:line, patch) | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SPC-F1 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/webui/settings/stedding_spaces_handler.cc:107 (0012, 0004) | The settings handler observes the SpaceModel of the window the settings tab first lived in. After the tab moves and that window closes, reloading or closing the tab calls RemoveObserver on a freed model; until then the page edits the old window's Spaces. | Observe the SpaceRegistry, resolve the model per message, and add OnSpaceModelDestroying with a check_empty observer list. |
| SPC-F2 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/spaces/space_model.cc:147 (0004) | SpaceModel caches a raw SessionService* that Close-profile deletes before the beforeunload prompts run. If the user picks Stay, the next Space switch, ⌘D or Move to Space writes through a freed pointer. | Drop the member and resolve GetForProfileIfExisting in one helper used by every write. |
| SPC-F3 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/browsing_data/chrome_browsing_data_remover_delegate.cc:1446 (upstream; 0046) | Delete browsing data clears only the default partition (plus IWA partitions). Isolated Space jars keep cookies, cache and storage while the dialog reports success. | Re-issue unfiltered removals for every registry entry with a profile_id from a Stedding hook. |
| SPC-F4 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/navigator/browser_navigator.cc:497 (0046, 0027) | A new tab's jar is chosen from the active Space before routing or pinning is known. The load starts before insertion, and a posted rebind later replaces the contents, dropping referrer, POST body and opener. target=_blank and noopener windows from an isolated Space start in the default partition. | Pick the routed Space's jar in CreateTargetContents, record it on the contents, and create renderer windows in the opener's partition. |
| SPC-F5 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/spaces/space_model.cc:653 (0004; 0019, 0046, 0049) | Every sleep and jar rebind replaces the WebContents under a new session id, and nothing re-writes its stedding.space, pin, home, split or tab name. A crash before the next rebuild loses them. | Handle kReplaced with SaveTabToSession and re-write the tab name. |
| SPC-F6 | critical | confirmed | content/browser/browsing_data/browsing_data_remover_impl.cc:826 (upstream; 0046) | Same gap as SPC-F3, seen from content: clearBrowserData, chrome.browsingData and site deletion never reach spaces/<token> partitions, including the jars of Spaces whose isolation was turned off. | Fan unfiltered removals out to every Space partition with a matching filter builder. |
| SPC-F7 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/webui/settings/stedding_spaces_handler.cc:107 (0012) | Duplicate of SPC-F1, traced independently: tear-off keeps the WebUI, and ~SpaceModel sends no destruction notice. | As SPC-F1; re-target on tab detach and insert if a model observation is kept. |
| SPC-F8 | critical | confirmed | chrome/browser/ui/views/frame/vertical_tab_strip_region_view.cc:1151 (0004) | SpaceTabVisibilityController holds a raw_ref to the TabStripView, which the View menu's switch to horizontal tabs deletes. #### Low-severity findings (not verified)

| ID | Where | Problem | Fix |
