| WEB-L113 | `chrome/browser/ui/webui/settings/import_data_handler.cc:266` | Closing the welcome before a browser import finishes silently skips the bookmark conversion. | Moot once the post-import conversion is removed (WEB-11); disable Finish while an import runs. |

### Platform, tooling and release

Nothing in this area explains the symptoms the owner reported (tabs that do not select on click, the crash, the rail's look). Both review passes found no tab, paint or animation code here, and no reachable CHECK. Those belong to the views and tab-strip areas. What this area does contain is a release blocker. The macOS profile migration in patch 0050 never runs in a real launch, because Crashpad creates `~/Library/Application Support/Stedding` before the migration checks whether that folder exists (PLT-F1). Every Mac user of betas 1 to 8 who installs the next release would open an empty browser, while `docs/INSTALL.md` tells them their data was copied and the old folder can be deleted. The migration's own unit tests pass because they call the helper on temporary folders, so `docs/features/windows.md` N8 says "built" for a feature that is dead. Around it: a new Stedding silently adopts Chromium's keychain key (PLT-F2), password-manager desktop integrations stopped working on macOS (PLT-F5), and on Windows the elevator and tracing interface IDs match no compiled interface, so system-level installs lose App-Bound Encryption (PLT-F6). The series adds no `browser_tests` or `interactive_ui_tests` at all (PLT-F8), and that is why startup-order and real-window bugs pass "green". The Mac signer (patch 0051) is sound. The tooling is well commented and its core works (apply-patches `--check`, update-patches, build-chromium's lock and fingerprint, the measure harness), but its verification layer reports green for things it never ran (PLT-T4, T5, T12, T13, T15), its destructive commands have no guards (PLT-T2, T7, T17), update-pin can move the pin backwards (PLT-T1), and the capture and drive tools can photograph or type into the owner's own Stedding window (PLT-T3). Nothing below was executed during the audit. "Confirmed" means traced in code, not reproduced.

#### Features

| Feature | Real state | Why (finding ids) | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| macOS profile in Stedding's own folder (windows N8, first half) | Works for fresh users; the folder is `~/Library/Application Support/Stedding` | Side effect: per-user native-messaging hosts moved with it (PLT-F5) | finish: keep, fix the side effect in PLT-5 |
| One-time migration of an old Stedding profile out of Chromium's folder (windows N8, second half) | Broken: never reached in a real launch; the helper is unit-tested only | PLT-F1, PLT-F3, PLT-F4, PLT-L6, PLT-L7; follow-up PLT-L3 | fix: rewrite (marker-based, before Crashpad, lock check, stage then rename). Set N8 back to `partial` until PLT-1's launch check passes |
| Keychain item under Stedding's name (import I24, patch 0038 fallback, shipped since beta 4) | Broken: old profiles decrypt when the legacy read succeeds, but a denied or failed read makes a new key and the data is lost (WEB-F2), and every new profile on a Mac with Chromium adopts Chromium's key and never writes its own | PLT-F2, WEB-F2 | fix: gate the fallback on migrated profiles, copy the key once |
| Native messaging hosts on macOS (KeePassXC, Bitwarden, 1Password desktop integration) | Broken since patch 0050: no vendor writes manifests into Stedding's folder | PLT-F5 | fix: fall back to Chromium's and Chrome's per-user folders |
| Windows install mode, per-user (windows N3, N6) | Works: folder, app id, ProgIDs, `stedding:` scheme, Active Setup, toast CLSID are consistent. Chromium's own install_static tests still assert Chromium's values and nothing runs them | PLT-F7, PLT-F9, PLT-T23 | fix: update the tests, run them in a Windows sweep |
| Windows elevation service and elevated tracing (system-level installs) | Broken: CoCreateInstance fails, App-Bound Encryption and elevated tracing are unavailable | PLT-F6 | fix: IIDs back to the implemented interfaces, or add Stedding interfaces to the IDLs |
| Windows policy root | Unverified: UserDataDir read from `Policies\Stedding`, every other policy from `Policies\Chromium` | PLT-L8 | simplify: decide in ADR 0018; if no own root, document that Chromium's policies apply |
| Mac Developer ID signing and notarization (patch 0051, `tooling/sign-release`) | Works; only `tooling/sign-release` assesses the stapled app | PLT-L9, PLT-T22 | finish: fix the comment and the notary-args echo |
| Build glue (BUILD.gn lists, paks, resource ids, `stedding_strings.grdp`) | Works for linking; hidden include cycles, duplicate peek dep, most UI strings hard-coded in English | PLT-F10, PLT-L4, PLT-L1, PLT-L2 | simplify: one Stedding-owned group target, strings into the grdp |
| End-to-end tests for Stedding behaviour | Missing: the series adds unit_tests only | PLT-F8 | finish: add browser_tests and interactive_ui_tests targets; every other area's acceptance checks depend on them |
| Pin tracking (`tooling/check-pin`, `tooling/update-pin`, upstream workflow) | Broken for the current early-stable pin: can report a false rollback and propose a downgrade | PLT-T1, PLT-T9 | fix: update-pin becomes a thin client of check-pin, served builds decide |
| Checkout and build tooling (sync-chromium, apply-patches, apply-branding, build-chromium, dev) | Partly: `tooling/dev build` crashes on stock macOS bash; force-checkout over uncommitted work; the build lock can vanish mid-build | PLT-T2, PLT-T6, PLT-T7, PLT-T16, PLT-T17, PLT-T18 | fix: shared guards in `tooling/lib.sh` |
| Branding (`tooling/apply-branding`, `tooling/brand/*`) | Partly: extension strings still say Chromium in every non-English locale; Windows incognito and other icons are Chromium's; `--check` passes with the strings reverted | PLT-T8, PLT-T19, PLT-T20 | fix |
| Test sweep (`tooling/dev test`) | Misleading: six "upstream" suites are not in unit_tests, six cited tests are in no filter, retries and zero-match filters pass | PLT-T4, PLT-T5, PLT-T15 | fix: filters derived from or checked against the spec rows |
| Capture and drive tools (`capture-state`, `capture-ui`, `capture-window.py`, `capture-windows`, `drive`, `drive-window.py`) | Unsafe: pick the browser by process name, so they can capture or drive the owner's real Stedding; drive reports "ok" on typos | PLT-T3, PLT-T14, PLT-T21 | simplify: one PID-based window helper; cut `capture-ui` and `capture-window.py` |
| Build verification (`tooling/verify-build`, `tooling/check-geometry`, `tooling/assert-capture`) | Proves less than it claims: full screen PASS means "alive 12 s", AV1 FAIL does not fail, CI measures pre-M155 screenshots | PLT-T12, PLT-T13, PLT-T25 | fix verify-build and assert-capture; cut check-geometry from CI unless it measures fresh captures |
| Release packaging and publishing (`package-dmg`, `package-installer.ps1`, `publish-release`) | Partly: nothing ties an image to the VERSION, commit, series, config or signature; betas ship the unmeasured `release` config while the docs say `official` | PLT-T10, PLT-T11 | fix: provenance file verified at publish, one shipping config in an ADR |
| Windows tooling (`tooling/win/*`) and CI | Partly: no pin, branding or lock checks on Windows; CI never runs macOS bash, most Python or any PowerShell | PLT-T23, PLT-T24 | fix |
| Linux profile folder and key name | Not started: would share Chromium's folder and secret-store key | PLT-L5 | defer to the Linux milestone; record it in the ADR now |

#### Work, in order

- [ ] **PLT-1** (P0) Rewrite the macOS profile migration so it runs before anything creates Stedding's folder, is recorded by a marker, refuses a live source and never nests or loses a copy. Fixes: PLT-F1, PLT-F3, PLT-F4, PLT-L6, PLT-L7. Steps: in `chrome/app/chrome_main_delegate.cc`, call the migration at the top of the IS_MAC block of `PreSandboxStartup`, before `InitMacCrashReporter`, for the browser process only (empty process type, no `--user-data-dir`, no UserDataDir policy). In `chrome/common/chrome_paths_mac.mm`: treat a target with no `Local State` and no profile folder (for example one holding only `Crashpad`) as not migrated; detect Stedding across every profile in the legacy `Local State` info cache, not only `Default`; read `legacy/SingletonLock` and skip (without marking done) when its host is this machine and its pid is alive; clone or copy into a staging folder, then `renamex_np(staging, target, RENAME_EXCL)`, deleting staging on EEXIST; never use `base::Move`; delete a stale staging folder on every launch; log every outcome; write the marker only after a successful copy or a deliberate decline; retry on later launches while the target has no user data; show a one-time notice naming the old folder when it fails. Keep `docs/INSTALL.md`'s "you can delete the old folder" out of any release until this passes. Done when: on a macOS user account holding a beta 8 profile in `~/Library/Application Support/Chromium` and no Stedding folder, the new build's first launch opens that profile's Spaces, pins and history, and the log names the migration outcome; the same happens when the Stedding folder already exists holding only `Crashpad` (the owner's current state); with a Chromium running on the legacy folder, nothing is copied, the notice appears, and the next launch after quitting Chromium migrates; a Chromium-only profile is not copied. The first case is also a PRE_ test in the browser_tests target from PLT-4.

- [ ] **PLT-2** (P0) Stop a fresh Stedding from reading Chromium's keychain secret, and end the dependency for migrated profiles after one launch. Fixes: PLT-F2. Depends on PLT-1's marker. In `components/os_crypt/common/keychain_password_mac.mm`, consult `Chromium Safe Storage` only when the marker says the profile came from a pre-rename Stedding; when it is used, write it into a new `Stedding Safe Storage` item. Correct `docs/INSTALL.md` in the same change. Done when: on a Mac with Chromium installed, a fresh Stedding profile raises no prompt for "Chromium Safe Storage" and Keychain Access shows a new "Stedding Safe Storage" item; a migrated beta 8 profile shows its saved passwords and stays signed in to a site after one launch, and still does after the "Chromium Safe Storage" item is deleted and Stedding relaunched.

- [x] **PLT-3** (P0) Replace the three window lookups in the capture and drive tools with one PID-based helper, and make drive fail loudly. Fixes: PLT-T3, PLT-T14, PLT-T21. Keep only windows whose `kCGWindowOwnerPID` is the launched pid or a descendant; activate and quit by pid; refuse to run while another process with the same bundle id is running; launch in the background (`open -g -n` or equivalent) and verify with a focus probe. Merge `tooling/capture-window.py` into `tooling/capture-windows`, fold `tooling/capture-ui` into `tooling/capture-state`, remove `drive-window.py`'s own lookup. In `drive-window.py`, exit non-zero on an unknown op, a failed shot or no window; in `tooling/drive`, put quit and kill in a trap. Done when: with an installed Stedding open on a personal page, `tooling/capture-state` and `tooling/drive` either refuse to start or capture and drive only the build they launched (the installed window is not in any PNG, receives no keys, keeps focus); a steps file with `clik 40 60` makes `tooling/drive` exit non-zero and leaves no browser running. **Checked 2026-09-28:** with a second instance of the same bundle id (`dev.stedding.Stedding`) running in the background, `tooling/capture-state` and `tooling/drive` both refuse to start and write no image; a steps file of `clik 40 60` makes `tooling/drive` exit 1 before anything is launched. `capture-window.py` is gone, `capture-ui` forwards to `capture-state`, and every window lookup goes through `window_by_pid.py`.

- [ ] **PLT-4** (P1, ordered here because every area's acceptance checks need it) Add a Stedding `browser_tests` target and an `interactive_ui_tests` target in a Stedding-owned BUILD file, wired into `chrome/test/BUILD.gn` by one line each, and run them from `tooling/dev test`. Fixes: PLT-F8. Cover at least: first launch with a legacy Chromium folder (PRE_ test, used by PLT-1), clicking a sidebar row activates its tab, switching Spaces by each entry point, entering and leaving immersive fullscreen with the sidebar open. Done when: `tooling/dev test` builds and runs both targets on the Mac, and a deliberately broken sidebar click handler (reverted afterwards) turns the click test red.

- [ ] **PLT-5** (P1) Restore password-manager desktop integrations on macOS by falling back to Chromium's and Chrome's per-user native-messaging folders. Fixes: PLT-F5. In `chrome/browser/extensions/api/messaging/launch_context_posix.cc`, after Stedding's own folder, search `~/Library/Application Support/Chromium/NativeMessagingHosts` and `~/Library/Application Support/Google/Chrome/NativeMessagingHosts`, guarded by `allow_user_level_hosts`, mirroring the Windows fallback; document the folder in `docs/FAQ.md`. Done when: with KeePassXC or Bitwarden desktop installed and its browser integration enabled for Chromium, the Stedding extension connects to the desktop app and fills a login on a real site, in a fresh Stedding profile.

- [ ] **PLT-6** (P1) Make the pin tooling unable to propose or apply a downgrade, and stop check-pin reporting a false rollback for an early-stable pin. Fixes: PLT-T1, PLT-T9. Give `tooling/check-pin` a `--json` output (state, latest) whose "serving" comes from versionhistory releases with no end time; a pin among the served builds is current. `tooling/update-pin` takes its target from that, compares with `version_key`, refuses a lower target without `--to X --allow-downgrade`, prints "downgrade" rather than "milestone bump", and fails on `--to` with no value. Add a self-test fixture for an early-stable pin with a later-dated respin of the previous milestone. Done when: `tooling/check-pin --self-test` includes that fixture and passes; running `tooling/update-pin` against a recorded feed where Mac stable's newest-by-date entry is 154.x reports "current" for the 155.0.8059.12 pin and `--apply` refuses to act.

- [ ] **PLT-7** (P1) Guard every command that rewrites the checkout, and make the build lock hold until the build is actually stopped. Fixes: PLT-T2, PLT-T7, PLT-T17. Add `require_no_build_running` and a dirty-tree and branch check to `tooling/lib.sh`; call them from `sync-chromium`, `update-pin --apply`, `apply-patches` (apply mode) and `apply-branding` (apply and revert); drop `checkout -f`; point the user to `apply-branding --revert` when only branding is dirty; delete dev's dead pgrep patterns. In `tooling/build-chromium`, split the traps: EXIT removes the lock, INT and TERM stop the build and exit. Done when: with an uncommitted edit in the checkout, `tooling/sync-chromium` and `tooling/update-pin --apply` refuse and the edit survives; during a running build, `tooling/apply-patches` and `tooling/apply-branding` refuse; `kill <build-chromium pid>` stops siso and a second `tooling/dev build` afterwards starts cleanly, while one started before the kill is refused.

- [ ] **PLT-8** (P1) Make the tooling run under macOS's stock bash 3.2 and have CI prove it. Fixes: PLT-T6, PLT-T24. In `tooling/build-chromium`, expand the positional array with the `${positional[@]+"${positional[@]}"}` guard; add a `check-repo shell` rule for unguarded `"${name[@]}"`; add a macos-latest CI job that runs `check-shell` under `/bin/bash` and each script's `--help`; parse all tooling Python (pyflakes or `ast.parse`) and every `.ps1`; add `tooling/lib.sh` to `series.yml`'s path filter. Done when: `/bin/bash tooling/dev build` and `tooling/build-chromium --budget 45` start a build on a stock Mac; a pull request that reintroduces the unguarded expansion, or a syntax error in `drive-window.py` or `build.ps1`, fails CI.

- [ ] **PLT-9** (P1) Tie every published image to the build that made it, and ship one documented config. Fixes: PLT-T10, PLT-T11. Record the shipping config in an ADR and make `package-dmg`, `sign-release`, the packager hint, `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `README.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` agree on it. `package-dmg` and `tooling/win/package-installer.ps1` read `stedding_version` from `out/<config>/args.gn`, refuse a mismatch with VERSION, and write `dist/<image>.provenance.json` (repo commit, hash of `patches/`, checkout HEAD and `refs/stedding/applied`, config, `apply-branding --check` result, signing mode). `publish-release` verifies it against HEAD and the ADR's config, refuses development-signed images, and drops the lone-image fallback. Done when: bumping VERSION without rebuilding makes `tooling/package-dmg` refuse; an image built with a config other than the ADR's, or signed with `--development`, is refused by `tooling/publish-release`; a correct image publishes and the About page of the installed build shows the tagged version.

- [ ] **PLT-10** (P1) Make the Windows elevator and tracing service reachable again, with real generated GUIDs. Fixes: PLT-F6, PLT-F9. In `chrome/install_static/chromium_install_modes.h` (patch 0041), either set `elevator_iid` and `tracing_service_iid` back to the implemented interfaces (`BB19A0E5-...` and `E0B03E2D-...`, keeping the fresh CLSIDs), or add `IElevator2Stedding` and `ISystemTraceSessionStedding` with `uuidgen` values to both IDLs and the RuntimeClass lists; if the IIDs change, list the beta 5 and 6 values in `old_elevator_iids` and `old_tracing_service_iids`. Regenerate the hand-typed GUIDs that have not shipped, keep shipped CLSIDs unless the old ones are also retired, write each GUID's string form in its comment, and run clang-format. Done when: a system-level install made with `mini_installer --system-level` starts, a browser log taken with `--enable-logging` shows no CoCreateInstance or QueryInterface failure for the elevator, cookies set on a real site survive a restart, and an elevated trace can be started.

- [ ] **PLT-11** (P2) Make Chromium's Windows identity tests assert Stedding's values and run them. Fixes: PLT-F7, PLT-T23. Update the non-branded expectations in `install_util_unittest.cc`, `user_data_dir_win_unittest.cc` and `product_install_details_unittest.cc`; add a check that `elevator_iid` is an interface the IDL declares; add `tooling/win/test.ps1` running unit_tests, `install_static_unittests` and `setup_unittests` from a filter table in a data file both platforms read; port build-chromium's pin, branding and lock checks into `tooling/win/build.ps1`; make `capture.ps1` use a fresh temporary profile and quote its arguments; cite the tests in `docs/features/windows.md` N3. Done when: on the Windows build machine, `tooling/win/test.ps1` runs and passes those suites, and reverting PLT-10's IID fix turns it red; `tooling/win/build.ps1` refuses a checkout off the pin or without branding.

- [ ] **PLT-12** (P2) Make `tooling/dev test` fail when it ran nothing, when a test only passed on retry, or when a cited test is in no filter. Fixes: PLT-T4, PLT-T5, PLT-T15. Split `upstream` into unit_tests suites and an `upstream-browser` group that runs browser_tests and interactive_ui_tests; list each pattern with `--gtest_list_tests` first and fail on any that matches nothing; pass `--test-launcher-retry-limit=0`; print the count run per feature; derive the per-feature filters from the spec rows' test column, or add a `check-repo` rule that every test in a "built" row matches its feature's filter, computing "defined" from the final series state. Done when: `tooling/dev test archive` and `tooling/dev test peek` run `ArchivePage*` and `PeekNewTab*`; renaming a test so its filter matches nothing makes `tooling/dev test` exit non-zero; a test made to fail on its first run only is reported as a failure.

- [ ] **PLT-13** (P2) Make the build probes measure what their PASS lines claim, and stop CI measuring stale screenshots. Fixes: PLT-T12, PLT-T13, PLT-T25. In `tooling/verify-build`, load the full-screen page from the local server and have it POST `document.fullscreenElement`, the inner-height check and a rAF tick after 10 s, failing if the POST is missing; count a required codec's FAIL as a failure; rename "navigation" to "network fetch" or navigate a real tab. Remove `tooling/check-geometry` from `checks.yml` (and the "looks at the product" claim), or run it in the release sweep on fresh `tooling/capture-state` output with coordinates from `geometry.json`. In `tooling/assert-capture`, reject probes with no assertion or rgb on a box (exit 2). Done when: a build started with `--start-fullscreen` removed (or a page that never enters full screen) makes `tooling/verify-build` report FAIL and exit non-zero; a probe file containing an assertion-free probe is rejected.

- [ ] **PLT-14** (P2) Finish the branding so no locale or Windows icon says Chromium, and make `--check` catch reverted strings. Fixes: PLT-T8, PLT-T19, PLT-T20. Add `extensions/strings/extensions_strings_*.xtb` to `tooling/brand/product_name.py`, rewriting only ids that belong to `extensions_chromium_strings.grdp`; map the remaining `win/*.ico` files and the intro animation; make `product_name.py --check` return 1 when anything would change and fold it into `apply-branding`'s status; have `generate.py` delete only files it writes, produce or list every asset `apply-branding` maps, and fix `branding/README.md`. Also: a private window's new tab page reads "Chromium won't save:" (`IDS_NEW_TAB_OTR_NOT_SAVED` in `components/new_or_sad_tab_strings.grdp`, and its translations in `components/strings/components_strings_*.xtb`), which no branding file covers (seen on a capture of the 155.0.8059.12 build, 2026-09-29). Done when: in a German-locale build, a private window's new tab page names Stedding, and an extension's context menu reads "Aus Stedding entfernen"; the Windows incognito shortcut shows Stedding's icon; `git checkout -- chrome/app/resources` followed by `tooling/apply-branding --check` exits non-zero.

- [ ] **PLT-15** (P3) Move the Stedding build glue into one Stedding-owned group and make `gn check` pass. Fixes: PLT-F10, PLT-L4. Drop the duplicate peek entry at `chrome/browser/ui/BUILD.gn:1589`; collect the Stedding targets and the welcome dialog sources into one group (for example `//chrome/browser/ui/stedding:all`) referenced once from `:ui` and unit_tests; split views targets that include `:ui` headers into a model part and a views part built by `:ui`, or declare `allow_circular_includes_from`; add `gn check` to `tooling/verify-build`. Done when: `gn gen --check out/release` passes and the next pin bump's `apply-patches --check` shows no conflict in the Stedding lines of `chrome/browser/ui/BUILD.gn`.

- [ ] **PLT-16** (P3) Move user-visible Stedding strings into the string table and use the platform's words. Fixes: PLT-L1, PLT-L2. Put every Stedding UI string in `chrome/app/stedding_strings.grdp` as `IDS_STEDDING_*`; add a `check-repo` rule rejecting capitalised `u"..."` literals in Stedding UI files; give the screenshot toast a "Show in Folder" variant chosen by `BUILDFLAG(IS_MAC)`. Done when: a Windows screenshot toast reads "Show in Folder"; running with `--lang=de` shows Stedding rows through the string table (English fallbacks are acceptable until translated) and the new rule passes.

- [ ] **PLT-17** (P3) Close the migration's follow-ups: web-app shims and the Linux profile. Fixes: PLT-L3, PLT-L5. After a successful PLT-1 migration, trigger the upstream shortcut rebuild for every installed web app so `CrAppModeUserDataDir` points at Stedding's folder; record in the ADR that Linux will rename its data dir and secret-store key the same way, with the same migration. Done when: a web app installed with beta 8 opens, after migration, in the main Stedding instance on the Stedding profile, with no second Stedding process and no Chromium attached.

- [ ] **PLT-18** (P3) Settle the Windows policy root and correct the signer comment. Fixes: PLT-L8, PLT-L9. Decide in ADR 0018 whether Stedding reads `Policies\Stedding`; if yes, generate the registry key from the branding and ship matching ADMX, if no, document that Chromium's policies apply. In `chrome/installer/mac/signing/signing.py`, run `spctl --assess` in the pipeline after stapling, or reword the comment to name `tooling/sign-release` as the only place the stapled app is assessed. Done when: on Windows, a policy set under the decided root (for example IncognitoModeAvailability) takes effect in Stedding and one set under the other root does not, matching the ADR; `sign_chrome.py --notarize staple` either prints a Gatekeeper result or its code no longer claims one.

- [ ] **PLT-19** (P3) Fix the small tooling faults. Fixes: PLT-T16, PLT-T18, PLT-T22. `sync-chromium`: check `re.subn`'s count, or re-run `gclient config` with the custom var, and fail when nothing changed. `apply-patches`: detect branding-only changes and say "run tooling/apply-branding --revert first"; `update-patches`: refuse commits that touch branding paths. `sign-release`: print only whether a keychain profile is in use, refuse `--password` in `STEDDING_NOTARY_ARGS`. Done when: `tooling/sync-chromium` on a `.gclient` with other custom vars either adds `checkout_pgo_profiles` or exits non-zero; `tooling/apply-patches` on a branded tree prints the revert hint; `tooling/sign-release --check` with a password in `STEDDING_NOTARY_ARGS` refuses and prints no password.

> **Note.** The two sections that follow come from the first pass of this audit and were not re-verified. The tests section judges every test the series adds, using the product-lens reviewers' reading of each one, and audits every feature spec row. The repository section holds the docs and patch-series audits. Treat their counts and findings as claims to check against the tree before acting (section 2). Their item ids (TST-, REPO-, SER-) are the ones section 3 uses.

### Tests and claims: what is actually proven

Two independent read-only passes covered every test the series adds (265 tests in 63 fixtures) and every behaviour row in the 34 feature specs under `docs/features/`. Nothing was built or run for this audit. "Exists" therefore means the test is declared at the pin's HEAD and compiled into a test target; it does not mean the test passes. Where the two readings of a test disagreed, this section counts the lower verdict.

Of the 265 Stedding tests:
- **85 (32%) are meaningful:** they would fail if the behaviour they name broke.
- **86 are weak:** they exercise real code, but the assertion or the fixture misses the case the row is about.
- **48 test a helper or a branch the product never uses.**
- **25 are tautological:** they assert what the code under test literally writes.
- **11 pin a bug:** they assert the wrong behaviour, so fixing the bug turns them red.
- **10 are broken:** they cannot test what their name says.

Even the 85 meaningful tests are all unit tests that stop at the model. No Stedding test clicks, hovers or drags a sidebar row through a widget. The series has no browser_tests or interactive_ui_tests of its own. It also adapts 12 upstream tests, five of them `TabTest` cases that now run with shipped features switched off. By reading, it leaves about a dozen upstream tests and suites red, and the Stedding sweep never runs them.

On the spec side, 274 of the 354 rows are claimed done:
- 222 rows name a test that exists.
- 132 rows cite only a capture, a live run, "code", or a test that does not exist.
- **About 120 rows** have a test that goes through the path a user takes: the window's own model, the navigator, or a command id. Even those stop short of the views.
- 106 rows are honestly marked model only, gap, planned or partial.

At least five test names cited in the specs do not exist, and six spec headers contradict their own tables.

Reading the code found broken behaviour in rows marked built, including the basics the owner hits:
- A hovered row in the collapsed rail shows a close button, and a click closes the tab.
- An abandoned rename or a favicon-reset click can leave rows in the open sidebar that do not respond.
- Collapsed folders reopen on the next Space switch.
- Folder trees reorder on restore.

The old rule, "a behaviour is shipped when its test is green", failed for a plain reason: most of those tests never touch the thing their row describes.

| Verdict | Tests | Share | What it means |
|---|---|---|---|
| meaningful | 85 | 32% | Would fail if the named behaviour broke; all are unit tests. |
| weak | 86 | 32% | Real code, but the assertion or the fixture misses the row's case. |
| helper-not-product | 48 | 18% | Tests a function or branch the shipped path does not use, or code with no caller. |
| tautological | 25 | 9% | Asserts a constant, a restated table, or an early return. |
| pins-bug | 11 | 4% | Asserts wrong behaviour; must be inverted when the bug is fixed. |
| broken | 10 | 4% | Cannot test what its name says (null context, seam, missing step). |

#### Why green tests did not mean working features

These are the patterns that let a feature pass its tests and still fail for the user. Each one names the tests where it shows.

1. **Model tests stood in for interaction claims.** Rows that promise something a user sees or does were proven by tests that never render, hover, click or restart.
   - `TabRowRulesTest.*` backs tabs R1–R4, R11, R18 and R19, but it tests boolean helpers and never lays out a `TabView`. The tests call the helper with `narrow=true`, and a real 44 DIP rail row never gets that value. So R3 is green while every hovered rail row shows a centred close button and a click closes the tab. This is the most likely cause of "tabs will not select on click" in the rail.
   - folders F3 cites `TabStripModelTest.*Hidden*`, which does not exist. The model's `IsTabHidden` is right, but `SpaceTabVisibilityController` shows a collapsed folder's rows again on every insert, move and Space switch.
   - `SpaceDragTargetTest.*` (spaces B12) and `FolderDragTargetTest.*` (folders F7) call the drop handler on a standalone model with a null view. The real drag detaches and reinserts the tab. That unpins essentials, erases Space pins and homes, and runs routing again.
   - `SpaceWindowTest.CloseOnPinnedTabSleepsIt` (pins H3) and `SpaceWindowTest.SleepOthersInSpaceSkipsActiveAndOtherSpaces` (tabs R5) claim "sleeps", but the fixture has no lifecycle unit, so no discard ever happens.
   - `RecentTabsSwitcherViewTest.ReleaseCommitsEscapeCancels` (switcher X3) feeds synthetic events to a view with no widget. On the Mac, a missed ⌃ release freezes the recent-tab history.
   - `AddressRowRulesTest.HiddenWithTheSidebarUnlessLocalhostOrOverridden` is a truth table cited for a layout and painting claim (toolbar T8).
   - `ShouldPeekTest.*` checks a pure predicate (peek P1), while the real peek lays out its page 0 px tall.
2. **Tests reached a helper, not the product.** Each test calls a function the product does not route through, or takes a branch production never takes.
   - `LittleWindowTest.CtrlDigitSendsToSpaceN` calls `PromoteLittleWindow` directly. In a real little window the command is disabled.
   - `SpaceWindowTest.UnpersistedRegistryIsolatesInMemory` builds `SpaceRegistry(nullptr)`, which production never builds (sessions S18).
   - Seven `SpaceModelTest` cases, `RenameAndRecolour` and `MoveSpaceReordersAndPersists` among them, cover the registry-less branch that only Blank Windows use.
   - `SessionRebuildTest.*` replays hand-written commands and never goes through `SessionServiceBase`.
   - `CommandBarViewTest.CmdLPrefillsTheUrlSelected` uses `PrefillForTesting` and never presses Enter. In the product, Enter opens a new tab (K12).
   - These tests cover code that has no production caller:
     - `ClipboardFenceTest.*`
     - `SidebarFileTest.*`
     - `SpaceWindowTest.CopyTabToIsolatedSpaceKeepsTheSourceJar`
     - `SpaceWindowTest.ThrowawayTabUsesAnInMemoryPartition`
     - `SpaceRouterTest.HostRouteForIsHostScoped`
     - `SpaceStoragePartitionTest.IsolationMarkFollowsProfileId`
     - `TabStripModelTest.MoveTabsToFolderAtInsertsBetweenChildren`
3. **Tautologies.**
   - `TabRowRulesTest.PlayingEssentialKeepsItsFavicon`: `ShowsFavicon()` returns true unconditionally.
   - `TabRowRulesTest.SleptRowDimsTitleAndIcon` asserts constants, while the product erases the icon dim.
   - `SteddingPrefsTest.EveryPreferenceRegistersAndDefaultsToOn` reads back what registration wrote.
   - `MenuRulesTest.PageMenuHidesGoogleRows` and `MenuRulesTest.AppMenuHidesGoogleRows` restate a switch statement.
   - `ShortcutReferenceTest.EverySteddingCommandWithAnAcceleratorIsListed` compares the rows with the lookup that built them.
   - `FolderDragTargetTest.DroppingNowhereLeavesTheTabAlone` and its `SpaceDragTargetTest` twin stop at an early return.
   - `SpaceStoragePartitionTest.WipeSharedSpaceIsNoOp` passes a null browser context, so it returns false for any Space.
   - `ArcDataImporterTest.UnsealsAV10Value` seals and unseals with the same assumptions about Arc.
4. **Tests that pin a bug.**
   - `TabRowRulesTest.PinnedAndCollapsedNeverShowClose`: the rail close button described above.
   - `SpaceWindowTest.SwitchingActivatesATabInTheTargetSpace` passes by strip order, because the last-used tab is never recorded (B3).
   - `SpaceModelTest.RefusesToRemoveTheActiveSpace` locks in a silent no-op behind an enabled Delete row.
   - `SpaceCommandTest.SpaceCommandsAreDisabledInPopupAndPrivateWindows` locks in the state that kills ⌃1–9 in little windows.
   - `TabArchiverTest.NeverArchivesTheActiveTab` expects a tab to be archived the moment the user leaves it.
   - `CopyLinkTest.PlainMarkdownAndHtmlFlavours` expects broken Markdown escaping.
   - `SpaceRegistryTest.ThrowawayIsInMemoryAndIsolated` asserts the persistence that throwaway T2 forbids.
   - `PrivacyDefaultsWindowTest.EveryDefaultIsTheProtectiveOne` passes Q2 while balanced HTTPS-First is off.
   - `TabMenuModelTest.SteddingOrder` pins an order that contradicts menus M1.
   - `SidebarBackupTest.SnapshotCarriesEveryField` pins an invented "Space 1" name.
   - `FolderViewTest.HeaderSurvivesANodeThatIsNotAFolder` builds a folder view over a tab node, which cannot happen. It enshrines the hack that hides a use-after-free.
5. **Fixtures that cannot reach the failure, and seams that replace the thing under test.**
   - The pin-sleep fixture has no essentials, so the wrap onto an essential (H11) never happens.
   - Folder fixtures are flat, or the nested folder is already its parent's last child, so the reorder bugs cannot show (`FolderSessionTest.RebuildsNestedFoldersFromParkedPaths`).
   - Command-bar fixtures have four tabs or fewer, so the result index equals the strip index (`CommandBarViewTest.ChoosingATabInAnotherSpaceSwitchesToIt`).
   - Some fixtures build states production never has:
     - a second tab in a little window (`TabArchiverTest.LittleWindowUsesItsOwnThreshold`);
     - an unpinned peek source (`PeekViewTest.PromotingIntoASplitPairsWithTheSourceTab`).
   - `NavigationSimulator` reports every navigation as not a link click, so `PeekThrottleTest.*` passes with the throttle deleted.
   - `RunUntilIdle` hides the turn in which a hidden tab is active (`SpaceWindowTest.ClosingTheLastTabOfASpaceOpensANewTabThere`).
   - These seams replace exactly what the row is about:
     - `SetPeekOpenForTesting`
     - `PrefillForTesting`
     - injected archive-source and restore lambdas
     - a fake accelerator provider
6. **Names and citations that promise more than the test asserts.**
   - `TabRowRulesTest.TitleWidthUnchangedByHover` never measures a width.
   - `PageThemeColorControllerTest.ClosingEveryTabReThemesAfterwards` asserts that nothing happens.
   - `AddressRowTest.ToggleOverridesUntilTheSidebarChanges` never changes the sidebar.
   - `SpaceModelTest.FocusHidesOtherSpaces` asserts no hiding.
   - These cited tests do not exist:
     - `TabStripModelTest.*Hidden*` (folders F3)
     - `SpaceModelTest.*Metadata*` (spaces B11)
     - `CommandBarViewTest.AtSpacePrefixFiltersOpenAndArchived` (new-tab N8)
     - `SpaceWindowTest.NumberedTabCountsASplitOnce` (splits J2)
     - "search.cc hunk" (new-tab N1), which is a source hunk, not a test
   - welcome W1 cites `SteddingPrefsTest`, which never touches the preference.
7. **Upstream tests went red, and nobody ran them.** By reading, the series breaks:
   - `DefaultSearchManagerTest.DefaultSearchSetByUserPref`
   - `DefaultSearchManagerTest.DefaultSearchOverridesIgnoredWhenFlagEnabled`
   - `TemplateURLPrepopulateDataTest.ClearProvidersFromPrefs` and the Google fallback cases
   - `RegionalCapabilitiesServiceProgramDeterminationTest` (`us_to_default`, `err_to_default`, `jp_to_default`)
   - `AcceleratorsCocoaBrowserTest.MappingAcceleratorsInMainMenu`
   - `VerticalTabStripRegionViewTest.NewWindowInheritsActiveWindowVerticalTabsState`
   - `install_static_unittests`
   - the `mini_installer` GUID expectations

   None of them is in the Stedding sweep.

#### The new definition of done and test strategy

A row is **verified** only when all four of these hold:

1. **The test exists and runs.** The test named in the row runs in the release sweep and is green on the current pin.
2. **The test goes through the user's path.** It enters where the user enters: an event dispatched through the widget, the command id, a real navigation, or a restart through a `PRE_` test. It uses no seam that replaces the thing the row is about. Its fixture is a state production can reach.
3. **The test was seen to fail.** Reverting or breaking the production line, or running the test before the fix, turned it red. The commit that adds the test says how.
4. **The acceptance step passed on a real build.** The feature's manual acceptance step passed on an installed build of the current pin, and the date and build are recorded in the spec.

Every other row is **unverified**. A row whose code has been removed is **cut**, and the spec says what was removed. No other states exist: "built", "partial", "model only", "live" and "capture" are retired. Captures prove pixels only and are never the test for a behaviour.

Each spec row carries these columns:

| Id | Behaviour | State | Test (product path) | Acceptance step | Last verified |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | One observable behaviour, in the user's words | verified, unverified or cut | `Suite.Case` and its target (unit_tests, browser_tests, interactive_ui_tests) | Script step number | Date and build, or blank |

A pure-rule unit test may be listed as supporting evidence. It may be a row's proof only when the row is itself a pure rule, such as the tracking-parameter table, the routing matcher or the drift comparison.

Which kind of test proves what:

| Kind | Proves | Does not prove | Required for |
|---|---|---|---|
| unit_tests, pure logic | Matchers, parsers, tables, arithmetic, formatting | Anything a user sees or does | Pure-rule rows only |
| unit_tests with a window model (`BrowserWithTestWindowTest`) | Model invariants across `TabStripModel`, `SpaceModel` and `SpaceRegistry`; commands through `chrome::ExecuteCommand` | Views, hit-testing, focus, restart, navigation throttles (`NavigationSimulator` never reports a link click) | Model invariants, and only with the full fixture below |
| views tests with a real `Widget` and `ui::test::EventGenerator` | Hit-testing, hover, click and drag inside one widget; laid-out bounds; visibility | Focus across windows, real keyboard delivery on the Mac | Every sidebar row, rail row, folder header, switcher chip and close-button claim |
| browser_tests (`InProcessBrowserTest`) | The real `Browser` and `BrowserView`; navigation against an embedded test server; session restore through `PRE_` tests; partitions and cookies; menus built by the real models; settings and welcome WebUI | Real input timing and focus | Restart and restore, isolation, peek, routing, command bar entry points, menus, settings |
| interactive_ui_tests | Real mouse and keyboard in a focused window: ⌃ release, Escape, ⌘ chords, drags between widgets, window activation | Anything that is not a UI flow | Clicking a row, switching Space by key, ⌃⇥, drag and drop |
| Manual acceptance script | What the owner sees on an installed build | Regressions between runs | Every feature, before every release |

Rules for all new tests:

- **One full fixture.** Every Spaces fixture has:
  - essentials;
  - a Space pin with a home URL;
  - a nested folder, one of them collapsed;
  - a split;
  - more than four tabs;
  - a second Space whose tabs are interleaved in strip order.

  A row about several windows is tested with two windows.
- **Nothing production cannot reach.** No tab node passed as a folder, no second tab in a little window, no `SpaceRegistry(nullptr)`, no unpinned peek source, and no null browser context standing in for a real profile.
- **No `RunUntilIdle` where a row says "never".** Attach an observer that checks the invariant at every `TabStripModel` notification.
- **Names match assertions.** If the name says "re-themes", "survives restart" or "restores to its Space", the test asserts exactly that.
- **Upstream suites around touched code run in the sweep.** This is trap 31. A red upstream test is either fixed in the product or adapted, with a comment naming the spec row that justifies the divergence.

The core flows each get a browser test or an interactive test before any other feature work:
1. Clicking a row selects it, in the open sidebar and in the rail.
2. Switching Space works through the chip, ⌃N, ⌥⌘←/→, the Spaces menu and the command bar.
3. Quitting and relaunching restores Spaces, pins, folders, splits and names.
4. A link opened from a tab inside a folder lands without a crash.
5. A tab dragged onto a folder header or a Space chip keeps what it had.
6. ⌘T and ⌘L followed by Enter do what the row says.

A manual acceptance script is 5–15 numbered steps per feature. It is written in the user's words and run by a person on an installed build before each release. Each step's result is recorded.

#### Spec by spec

"Test exists" means a named automated test declared at HEAD and compiled into a test target; it was not run for this audit. "Proves product path" is the reader's judgement that the test drives the path a user takes. None of these tests reaches the views through real input.

| Spec | Rows | Claimed done | Test exists | Proves product path | Model-only/gap | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| spaces.md | 32 | 31 | 26 | 16 | 1 | **Overstated.** The model is real and mostly tested through the window's own `SpaceModel`. B11 cites a test pattern that matches nothing. B16, B17, B23, B26 and B30 have no test. B12 is tested on a standalone model. Dropping a tab on a chip unpins essentials and erases pins and homes (B6, B12). Delete on the active Space is enabled and does nothing (B5, B16). B3's test passes by strip order. |
| sessions.md | 19 | 16 | 19 | 12 | 3 | **Overstated.** The partition plumbing is real. S18 is tested on a registry that production never builds. S11's test cannot tell the claimed rule from the default. S13's shared-Space test is vacuous. Routed and external tabs send their first request from the wrong jar. Isolation toggles and wipes reach only the calling window. |
| tabs.md | 22 | 21 | 19 | 8 | 1 | **Overstated.** Most row tests are pure rules, and the header contradicts the table. R19 counts a split twice. The ⌘T rows promised in R5 and R14 do not exist. R6's `SetAutoDiscardable` is never called. R20/R21: a shift-click selects hidden tabs, and the verbs then close or move them. R14's field never ends on blur. |
| folders.md | 13 | 12 | 11 | 7 | 1 | **Overstated.** The model operations are real. F3 cites a test that does not exist, and the view reopens collapsed folders. F5 reorders nested folders on restore. Any insert at an index inside a folder hits a CHECK. |
| pins.md | 12 | 12 | 10 | 4 | 0 | **Overstated.** The home-URL bookkeeping is real, but "sleeps" is never proven: there is no lifecycle unit and no essentials in the fixture. H6's ⌘T half does not exist, and Make This the Pinned Page is reverted on restored pins. H11 wraps onto an essential. H5/H10 are live-only, and the favicon reset leaks the drag state. |
| splits.md | 6 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 4 | **Partly true.** J1 and J4 are tested at model level. J1's restore goes through a test helper and can re-split a pair the user unsplit. J2 is not implemented, although tabs R19 says it is. |
| switcher.md | 5 | 5 | 5 | 3 | 0 | **Partly true.** X1, X4 and X5 go through real commands. X3 uses synthetic events without a widget. A missed ⌃ release freezes the history. |
| sidebar.md | 14 | 7 | 6 | 4 | 7 | **Overstated.** Density and text size are real. Y3 and Y6 are capture-only. Y5's test asserts the opposite of its claim. Rail rows swap the favicon for a close button, and a click closes the tab (Y6, Y8, Y12). Favicons ignore the density preset. |
| motion.md | 3 | 2 | 3 | 0 | 1 | **False in part.** Only the helper is tested. The ⌘-badge and hover-card fades ignore the setting. The header contradicts the table. |
| focus.md | 3 | 2 | 2 | 0 | 1 | **Overstated.** A model flag and a predicate only. ⌥⌘←/→ and the swipe leave Focus silently. Focus has no chord. |
| share-coat.md | 3 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 2 | **Overstated.** A one-line predicate. ⌘T still lists every Space's tabs while the coat is on. |
| throwaway.md | 4 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 4 | **Honest.** Nothing calls it. Its one test pins the persistence that T2 forbids. |
| clipboard.md | 3 | 0 | 2 | 0 | 3 | **Honest.** Dead code with tests; nothing is fenced. |
| library.md | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 7 | **Honest.** Planned. |
| player.md | 4 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 4 | **Honest.** Planned. |
| routing.md | 10 | 8 | 10 | 5 | 2 | **Partly true.** The matchers are real. D6 tests a parameter no caller passes. D2's test bypasses the navigator. A first rule pointing at a missing Space blocks later rules. Settings removes a rule by a stale index. A drag runs routing again. |
| windows.md | 14 | 6 | 6 | 5 | 8 | **Overstated.** The shared Space list is real and tested with two windows. G1 claims shared essentials and pins, but they are per window. A Blank Window merges into the registry after a restart (G4). |
| import.md | 25 | 22 | 19 | 9 | 8 | **Overstated.** The parser and apply steps have run only on a synthetic file. Re-import resurrects a deleted Space and skips tabs added inside known folders. Backup restore duplicates every open tab after a restart (I19). A denied keychain read writes a new key (I24). History duplicates on re-import. |
| menus.md | 7 | 7 | 6 | 4 | 3 | **Overstated.** The menus do consult the rules. `SteddingOrder` pins an order that contradicts M1. There is no Move to Folder submenu. M6 has no test. |
| archive.md | 11 | 11 | 9 | 6 | 3 | **Overstated.** The sweep is real, but the idle clock resets at every launch and pauses while the machine sleeps. A9 and A10 restore is tested only through stubs. A little window can never be archived. |
| commandbar.md | 19 | 17 | 14 | 8 | 6 | **Overstated.** The tests run production methods on a view that is never shown, often through seams. Enter can open a row other than the highlighted one (K5). ⌘L then Enter opens a new tab (K12). Some registry action rows crash when run (K9). |
| toolbar.md | 25 | 17 | 10 | 2 | 9 | **Unproven.** Layout and painting rows are proven by boolean tables. The focused field and dropdown use a truncated colour (T20). The row goes stale on localhost navigation (T11). Private windows get none of it. |
| privacy.md | 11 | 9 | 7 | 6 | 3 | **Partly true.** The Q1–Q3 and Q5–Q7 defaults are read from a real profile, but Q2 is not in effect (`IsBalancedModeEnabled` ignores it), and the mechanism Q4 describes does not exist. |
| shortcuts.md | 6 | 5 | 4 | 3 | 1 | **Overstated.** The tests compare the accelerator table with itself. The reference lists little-window keys that do nothing. |
| welcome.md | 9 | 8 | 2 | 1 | 2 | **Unproven.** Everything except W7 is live-only. W1 cites a test that never touches its preference. W7 tints the active Space, not the first. |
| peek.md | 10 | 10 | 10 | 7 | 3 | **Overstated.** The cancel-and-open-a-peek path is never tested. The peek's page is laid out 0 px tall. The throttle tests pass with the throttle deleted. Server redirects escape the peek. |
| little.md | 5 | 4 | 4 | 1 | 3 | **False.** ⇧⌘O, Escape and ⌃1–9 are disabled in a real little window (E2), and a little window can never be archived (E3). The tests call helpers directly. |
| copy-link.md | 6 | 6 | 4 | 3 | 1 | **Mostly true.** The writer is tested on a real clipboard. The Markdown test pins broken escaping. The header contradicts the rows. |
| private.md | 6 | 4 | 3 | 2 | 3 | **Partly true.** V2's test checks only that there is no `SpaceModel`. The graphite heuristic also catches normal windows that use the grayscale theme. |
| settings.md | 12 | 10 | 4 | 2 | 4 | **Unproven.** Mostly live-only. T7's slider rewrites dragged widths. Deleting the active Space in T9 does nothing. chrome://settings/importData does not exist (I13). |
| screenshot.md | 7 | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **Unproven.** No tests at all. |
| new-tab.md | 8 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 3 | **Unproven.** No tests. N8 cites a test that does not exist, and N1 cites a source hunk. |
| page.md | 8 | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **Unproven.** No tests. The header contradicts the rows. |
| sidebar-file.md | 5 | 0 | 2 | 0 | 5 | **Honest rows, false prose.** The module has no caller. The "Export sidebar…" row the prose mentions does not exist. The export that does exist writes every unpinned tab with no opt-in. |
| **Total** | **354** | **274** | **222** | **about 120** | **106** | |

#### Tests to delete or rewrite

The unit names are the audit's reading units. "Delete" means removing the test and every spec citation of it. When a test covers code with no production caller, it goes out with that code if `S-61` withdraws it; if the code is wired instead, the test is rewritten through the surface. The TST numbers refer to the work list below.

| Test | Unit | Verdict | Why | Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TabRowRulesTest.PinnedAndCollapsedNeverShowClose` | sidebar-views | pins-bug | The test feeds `narrow=true`, which a real rail row never gets. It stays green while hovered rail rows show a close button and a click closes the tab. | Rewrite as a views test: lay out a real `TabView` at rail width, hover it, assert there is no close button, click it, assert it is selected (TST-2). |
| `FolderViewTest.HeaderSurvivesANodeThatIsNotAFolder` | sidebar-views | pins-bug | It builds a folder view over a tab node, which cannot happen, and hides the freed-node use-after-free. | Delete. Add a test that destroys the folder node while its view is alive, under ASan (TST-7). |
| `SpaceWindowTest.SwitchingActivatesATabInTheTargetSpace` | spaces-model | pins-bug | It passes by strip order. `last_active_` is never set for a foreground-inserted tab. | Rewrite: the last-used tab is not first in strip order and was inserted in the foreground (TST-3). |
| `SpaceModelTest.RefusesToRemoveTheActiveSpace` | spaces-model | pins-bug | The UI offers Delete on the active Space, and this refusal turns it into a silent no-op. | Rewrite after the owner decides the behaviour (switch, then delete; or disable the row), through the Space menu and settings (TST-12). |
| `SpaceCommandTest.SpaceCommandsAreDisabledInPopupAndPrivateWindows` | spaces-registry-sessions | pins-bug | It locks in the state that makes ⌃1–9 unreachable in little windows. Its no-crash half runs Clear on an empty popup, whereas a real private window closes. | Rewrite after little E2 and spaces B29 are reconciled. Add a private-window Clear case (TST-10, TST-12). |
| `SpaceRegistryTest.ThrowawayIsInMemoryAndIsolated` | spaces-registry-sessions | pins-bug | It asserts that a throwaway Space survives a prefs reload, the opposite of T2, and never checks `isolated`. | Delete with `AddThrowawaySpace` (S-61), or invert it if throwaway is built. |
| `TabArchiverTest.NeverArchivesTheActiveTab` | archive | pins-bug | It expects a tab that was active for 13 hours to be archived the moment the user leaves it. | Rewrite: the idle time starts at deactivation and survives a restart (TST-18). |
| `CopyLinkTest.PlainMarkdownAndHtmlFlavours` | stedding-helpers-a | pins-bug | It expects `[A [Page\]](...)`, which CommonMark renders as the text "[A " followed by a link on "Page]". | Rewrite with correct escaping, checked by parsing the output as CommonMark. Keep the real-clipboard read. |
| `TabMenuModelTest.SteddingOrder` | commands-menus-keys | pins-bug | It pins the code's order, which contradicts menus M1, and lists Pin and Unpin rows that never show together. | Rewrite from M1 once the owner confirms the order. Build the menu for a real tab of each kind. |
| `SidebarBackupTest.SnapshotCarriesEveryField` | import | pins-bug | It asserts that the unnamed default Space exports as "Space 1". | Rewrite: an unnamed Space exports with no name. |
| `PrivacyDefaultsWindowTest.EveryDefaultIsTheProtectiveOne` | stedding-helpers-b | pins-bug | Q2 passes while `IsBalancedModeEnabled()` is false. The Q6 checks assert upstream's own defaults. | Rewrite to assert the effective state, and drop Q6 (TST-21). |
| `TabRowRulesTest.TitleWidthUnchangedByHover` | sidebar-views | broken | The name promises a width, but the test asserts booleans. | Rewrite as a views test that measures the title's bounds with and without hover. |
| `SpaceStoragePartitionTest.EmptyProfileIdIsNoPartition` | spaces-registry-sessions | broken | The non-empty case returns nullopt only because the browser context is null. | Rewrite with a `TestingProfile`. |
| `AddressRowTest.ToggleOverridesUntilTheSidebarChanges` | stedding-helpers-a | broken | It never collapses or expands the sidebar. | Rewrite: toggle, collapse, expand, and assert the override resets (TST-20). |
| `PageThemeColorControllerTest.ClosingEveryTabReThemesAfterwards` | stedding-helpers-a | broken | It asserts that nothing happens; `saw_closing_all` can never be true. | Rewrite to assert the re-theme after closing every tab (TST-20). |
| `CommandBarViewTest.ArchivedRowRestoresToItsSpace` | commandbar | broken | Both the archive source and the restore are lambdas, so no Space is involved. #### Work, in order

- [ ] **TST-1** (P0) Click a row in the open sidebar and prove it selects.
  - Add an interactive_ui_tests suite with a real window and a real mouse.
  - Click the title, the favicon and the blank area of an inactive row. Cover each row kind: an unpinned tab, a Space pin, an essential card, a tab in a folder, a tab in a nested folder, and a split row.
  - Repeat after a rename was started and abandoned by clicking elsewhere, and after a favicon click that reset a drifted pin.
  - Assert that the clicked tab becomes active, that the next click still works, that a tab can still be dragged, and that the window closes.

  Fixes:
  - the owner's report that tabs will not select on click. Its state is unknown: the static path reads clean, so it needs this instrumented click.
  - the rename field that never ends on blur and swallows clicks on its row (tabs R14).
  - the favicon reset that skips `EndDrag`, leaving the window unable to close and fullscreen refused until the next row click (pins H5/H10).

  Done when:
  - the suite runs in the sweep;
  - either it reproduces the owner's failure, or the owner's exact steps are added as a case until it does;
  - it is red before the fixes and green after;
  - tabs R14 and pins H5/H10 cite it. **Status 2026-09-29, patch 0057:** in place, as browser_tests on real rows (the sweep runs `stedding_browser_tests`): `SidebarClickTest.EveryPartOfEveryRowSelects` clicks the favicon, the title and the empty end of the title's line of every kind of row -- plain, Space pin, essential card, folder, nested folder, both panes of a split -- in the open sidebar and in the rail, then drags a row and closes the window; `AClickOnAnotherRowEndsAnOpenRename`, `AClickOnThePageEndsARename` and the other rename cases repeat after an abandoned rename; `RowsSelectAfterAPinGoesHome` and `AfterAPinGoesHomeTheWindowCloses` after a favicon reset. Each fix was seen red by taking it out (the rename watcher, the drag ended first). Tabs R14 and pins H5 cite them. Open: the owner's own failure, which none of these reproduces: the owner's exact steps are needed as a case.
- [x] **TST-2** (P0) Hover and click in the collapsed rail.
  - Write a views test with a real `Widget` and `EventGenerator`, plus one interactive case.
  - Collapse the sidebar and hover each row kind. Assert that the favicon stays and no close button is visible.
  - Click each row and assert it is selected, never closed.
  - Click a drifted Space pin's favicon and assert the row is selected.
  - Drag a Space chip a few pixels in the rail stack.
  - This test replaces `TabRowRulesTest.PinnedAndCollapsedNeverShowClose`.

  Fixes:
  - every hovered rail row showing a centred close button, and a click closing the tab (sidebar Y6/Y8/Y12, tabs R1–R3);
  - a rail favicon click resetting the pin instead of selecting it (pins H5);
  - the drift dot clipped in the rail (H4);
  - any jitter in a rail chip drag moving that Space to the top (spaces B27).

  Done when it is red on the current series and green after the fixes, and every rail and close-button row cites it. **Proven 2026-09-29, patch 0057,** by browser_tests with a real window, dispatching through its widget: `SidebarClickTest.AHoveredRailRowShowsNoCloseButton` (every kind of row hovered in the rail: favicon in place, no close button; each click selects and closes nothing), `SidebarClickTest.ARailClickKeepsADriftedPinsPage` (a drifted Space pin's and essential's favicon click selects and keeps the page), `SpaceSwitchTest.AShakyClickOnAChipMovesNothing` (a two-pixel chip drag in the rail stack moves nothing). The fixes were in the series before these were written; with the rail rule taken out (`IsRailRow` false) the hover and dot cases go red. Tabs R3, pins H4, H5, sidebar Y8 and spaces B27 cite them; `TabRowRulesTest.PinnedAndCollapsedNeverShowClose` stays as the rule's unit test.
- [x] **TST-3** (P0) Switch Space by every path a user has.
  - Write a browser test, with interactive cases for keys and the swipe. Switch through the chip, ⌃N, ⌥⌘←/→, the Spaces menu and a command-bar tab row.
  - Use the full fixture from the strategy above, and make the last-used tab a foreground-inserted tab that is not first in strip order.
  - Assert:
    - the switch lands on the tab last used in the target Space;
    - an invariant observer never sees a hidden active tab at any notification;
    - a collapsed folder's rows stay hidden, and other Spaces' folder headers are hidden;
    - closing a Space's last tab never activates a hidden tab;
    - a switch in horizontal-tab mode does not crash.
  - This test replaces `SpaceWindowTest.SwitchingActivatesATabInTheTargetSpace` and `SpaceWindowTest.ClosingTheLastTabOfASpaceOpensANewTabThere`.

  Fixes:
  - spaces B3: the last-used tab is not recorded, and an active essential short-circuits the switch;
  - B4: a hidden tab is active for a turn;
  - B1 view half: folder headers show in every Space;
  - folders F3: collapsed folders reopen;
  - the visibility controller's use-after-free in horizontal mode.

  Done when it is red now and green after the fixes, and spaces B1–B4 and folders F3 cite it. **Proven 2026-09-29, patch 0057:** `SpaceSwitchTest.NoSwitchEverShowsAHiddenTab` switches by ⌃N (the Spaces menu runs the same commands), next and previous, a chip, a command-bar tab row and a two-finger swipe (scroll events through the window), with a folder collapsed in Two and Two's last-used tab opened in the foreground after the others; a watcher checks every notification of the strip and of the Spaces. It was red on the series: closing a Space's last tab left another Space's tab active for a turn (spaces B4), fixed the same day, and it fails again with the fix taken out. `SpaceKeysTest.EachChordRunsItsSpaceCommand` takes ⌃1-9 and ⌥⌘←/→ through the Mac's key path to their commands; `SpaceSwitchTest.SwitchingWithHorizontalTabs` switches with horizontal tabs. Spaces B1, B3, B4, B15, B18, B19 and folders F3 cite them.
- [ ] **TST-4** (P0) Restart and restore, proven with `PRE_` browser tests.
  - Build a profile with:
    - three Spaces, one of them isolated;
    - essentials;
    - Space pins with homes;
    - a nested folder tree with two nested siblings in the middle of a parent, one of them collapsed and renamed;
    - a split, plus a pair the user unsplit;
    - a renamed tab, and a renamed tab that is asleep.
  - Restart, then assert every item by identity and order.
  - Unpin one restored pin, set a new home on another, restart again, and assert both changes held.
  - ⌘⇧T and Reopen Closed Window bring a tab back to its Space and jar with its name.
  - A Blank Window comes back blank.
  - These tests replace, as the proof for their rows, `SessionRebuildTest.*`, `FolderSessionTest.RebuildsNestedFoldersFromParkedPaths`, the restore half of `SpaceWindowTest.SplitPinRenameAndRestoreKeepThePair`, and `SpaceWindowTest.RestoredTabInIsolatedSpaceUsesItsPartition`.

  Fixes:
  - folders F5: nested folders sink to the end, siblings regroup, and rename and collapse are not saved;
  - spaces B2, B9, B10 and B21, and pins H2 and H6, on restored tabs;
  - tabs R16/R17: the name is lost on sleep and on ⌘⇧T;
  - archive A8, sessions S12 and windows G4;
  - splits J1, which re-splits an unsplit pair.

Open: a Blank Window coming back blank, and the rows this item names citing the PRE_ test (folders F5, spaces B2, B9, B10, B21, pins H2, H6, tabs R16, R17, archive A8, sessions S12, windows G4, splits J1).

  - For each one, either fix the product or adapt the test with a comment naming the spec row.

  Done when the release sweep runs all of them green.
- [ ] **TST-15** (P2) Browser tests for peek.
  - Click a real cross-site link in a pinned tab (not through `NavigationSimulator`) and assert that a peek opens with a page of non-zero height.
  - Cover `target=_blank`, `window.open` with an opener, and ⌘-click.
  - Cover a same-site link that server-redirects to another site.
  - Promote with ⌘O and ⇧⌘O from a Space pin and from an essential, including in an isolated Space, with no reload.

  Fixes peek P1, P6, P7, P8, P9 and the redirect escape; routing D6.

  Done when `PeekThrottleTest.*` and `PeekViewTest.PeekLoadsItsOwnPage` are replaced.
- [ ] **TST-16** (P2) Browser tests for the little window on the Mac.
  - An external URL opens a little window through the app-controller path.
  - ⇧⌘O, Escape and ⌃1–9 act on it.
  - A one-tab little window is archived after its own threshold.
  - The mark survives a restore.

  Fixes little E1, E2, E3 and E5, and the conflict with spaces B29.

  Done when `LittleWindowTest.*` and `TabArchiverTest.LittleWindowUsesItsOwnThreshold` are replaced.
- [ ] **TST-17** (P2) Browser tests for isolation, against an embedded server that records cookies.
  - A routed tab, an external open and a noopener popup send their first request from the target Space's jar.
  - Turning isolation on or off rebinds tabs in every window.
  - Clear Independent Session and Delete Space wait for the cache clear and remove the directory.
  - Wiping an in-memory jar never touches disk.
  - A Blank Window hides the isolation controls.

  Fixes sessions S4, S7, S10, S13, S17 and S18, and routing D2.

  Done when the partition rows cite these tests.
- [ ] **TST-18** (P2) Tests for archive.
  - Persist the idle time and test it across a restart and a simulated system sleep.
  - A tab the user just left is not archived at once.
  - A restored tab goes back by Space id after a rename and a reorder, into its folder and its jar.
  - The archive page lists entries after a restart.

  Fixes archive A1, A2, A8, A9 and A10.

  Done when `TabArchiverTest.NeverArchivesTheActiveTab`, `ArchivePageWindowTest.RestoreRecreatesAMissingSpace` and `CommandBarViewTest.ArchivedRowRestoresToItsSpace` are replaced.
- [ ] **TST-19** (P2) Tests for import and backups.
  - Use a sanitised real Arc sidebar file as a fixture, including split views and custom titles.
  - Re-import after deleting a Space, and after Arc added a tab inside a known folder.
  - A second history import adds no visits.
  - Restoring a backup after a restart duplicates nothing.
  - The ⌘T import shows a result.
  - Converting bookmarks to pins hides the bar and the star, and the change can be undone.

  Fixes import I1, I12, I14, I19, I21, I22 and I24.

  Done when the broken and tautological import tests listed above are replaced.
- [ ] **TST-20** (P2) Tests for the toolbar and the page colour, on a laid-out `BrowserView`.
  - When the sidebar collapses, the address row hides and the card takes its height (T8).
  - Navigating to and from localhost updates the row (T11).
  - The ⇧⌘D override resets on the next collapse (T10).
  - The focused field and the dropdown use the exact page colour (T20).
  - Switching to a loading tab does not keep the old colour (T3/T21).
  - Immersive full screen paints the row.
  - Private windows behave as the spec says.

  Done when the `AddressRow*`, `PageBarColorSupplierTest`, `PageThemeColorControllerTest` and `SteddingWindowBackgroundTest` cases listed above are rewritten.
- [ ] **TST-21** (P2) Browser tests for settings, welcome and privacy.
  - Settings: deleting the active Space, a slider that does not rewrite a dragged width, chrome://settings/importData, and the isolation toggle.
  - Welcome: shown once, and Skip, without `--no-first-run`.
  - Privacy: Sec-GPC is sent after a restart with the preference on, and balanced HTTPS-First is actually in effect.

  Fixes settings T7 and T9, import I13, welcome W1 and W2, and privacy Q2 and Q4.

  Done when `PrivacyDefaultsWindowTest`, `PrivacyDefaultsTest.GpcSwitchFollowsTheLocalState` and `SteddingPrefsTest` are rewritten.
- [ ] **TST-22** (P2) Write acceptance scripts for every other feature spec.
  - Start with screenshot.md, new-tab.md and page.md, which have no automated tests.
  - Run them on an installed build before each release and record the results.

  Done when every spec has a script and a recorded run.
- [ ] **TST-23** (P2) Windows.
  - Adapt `install_static_unittests` and the `mini_installer` expectations to Stedding's Windows identity.
  - Run the sweep on the Windows build machine for every Windows release (S-56).

  Done when both suites are green on Windows.
- [ ] **TST-24** (P3) Make "seen red" part of the loop.
  - Every new test's commit records how it was made to fail.
  - Run a mutation pass over `space_model.cc`, the two drag targets and the visibility controller to find assertions that do not bite.

  Done when `docs/AGENT-LOOP.md` requires the red-first step and the first mutation pass is recorded.
- [ ] **TST-25** (P3) Clean up the remaining weak pure-logic tests.
  - Rename the tests whose names overclaim, for example `SpaceQueryTest.AtSpacePrefixFiltersOpenAndArchived`, `SpaceModelTest.FocusHidesOtherSpaces` and `TabArchiverTest.SweepRecordsSpaceAndReason`.
  - Add the missing cases, such as opaque-origin homes and more than five tabs for ⌃⇥, where the rule is worth keeping.
  - Remove them as proof from any user-facing row.

  Done when no weak test is the sole citation of a row.

### The repository: docs, patch series and layout

> **First pass, not re-verified** (see the note before the tests section). Its findings table has no state column for that reason.

The documentation describes a more finished and better-tested browser than the one in the tree. The pages people read (README, INSTALL, SHORTCUTS, FAQ, PRIVACY and the release notes) state as fact things the shipped build does not do. They call Safe Browsing on and protective, though no API key stands behind it. They say the browser does not contact Google, though idle profiles reach Google for three services. They offer a Windows installer in a release that has none, name a profile folder that only an unreleased build uses, and give a build command that stops itself after 15 minutes. The internal docs fail at their one job, which is to tell the next person what is true. The worst open bug (tabs cannot be selected by clicking) is recorded only in the feedback ledger. Four test fixtures that closed backlog items are run by no sweep. ADRs say work was done that was not, and the roadmap marks six milestones done against criteria nobody measured. The project's state is retold, differently each time, in AGENTS, BACKLOG, HANDOFF and ROADMAP. The patch series applies and builds at its tip, and `patches/` matches the working branch byte for byte, but it is not the minimal-patch fork the ADRs promise:
- 52 patches over 405 files, of which 201 are upstream files carrying 6,923 changed lines;
- 71 upstream files edited by more than one patch;
- four patches that do not build on their own;
- 204 lines of pure reformatting;
- upstream defaults and upstream tests edited in place;
- one real bug that the structure review found: patch 0038's keychain fallback.

Most of the product (28,123 lines) sits in new files, which is why a much smaller upstream footprint is within reach. The order of repair is: first stop telling users things that are false, then make the tests mean something, then fix the bugs, and only after that re-cut the series.

#### Target layout

User-facing paths stay where they are, because stedding.dev and outside links point at them. Each kind of state lives in one place: BACKLOG for work, `docs/features/` for behaviour, `docs/release-notes/README.md` for releases. History lives only in `docs/history/` and `docs/release-notes/`. The two audits placed the build guide in two different spots (`docs/BUILDING.md` and `docs/dev/BUILD.md`) and the competitor page in two (`docs/project/` and `docs/research/`). This plan keeps one of each: `docs/dev/BUILD.md` and `docs/research/`.

```
README.md                            front door, 150 lines at most: pitch, one current capture, generated Download table, top shortcuts, true privacy bullets, platform status, links
AGENTS.md                            about 100 lines: mandate, hard rules, where things live, the one definition of done, how to verify, pointers; no history, dates or counts
CLAUDE.md                            pointer to AGENTS.md (unchanged)
BACKLOG.md                           the only record of open work: strictly ordered, 3 lines a row at most, each row naming its failing test or its check
CONTRIBUTING.md                      short: every CI job with the local command that reproduces it, the Windows tooling, a link to docs/dev/BUILD.md
SECURITY.md                          how to report; links to INSTALL.md for signing status and the quarantine step
LICENSE                              unchanged
VERSION                              the version this tree builds; a -dev string between releases, moved with every pin move
.github/workflows/checks.yml         repo checks: check-repo, check-shell, check-pin --self-test; check-geometry only if the owner keeps it (section 2's cuts, PLT-13)
.github/workflows/series.yml         applies the series to the pin, compares patches/series.tree, runs boundary rules B1-B7
.github/workflows/upstream.yml       unchanged
branding/                            unchanged; branding/README.md is the visual system BRAND.md points to
patches/README.md                    the one organisation rule, the boundary rules, the upstream footprint per file
patches/MANIFEST                     every path the series touches, mapped to the one patch that owns it
patches/series.tree                  the tree id the series must reproduce on the pin
patches/00NN-<area>-<summary>.patch  18 patches in 6 layers (see The patch series)
tooling/README.md                    index of every script, including win/, verify/, measure/, probes/ and the capture tools
tooling/                             scripts; build-chromium's 15-minute budget applies only when an agent flag or variable is set
docs/README.md                       index in four groups: people, product, contributors, history
docs/INSTALL.md                      steps per platform, which release carries which asset, data folders and keychain items per release, uninstall, the only signing-status sentence
docs/SHORTCUTS.md                    generated from ShortcutReference() for macOS and Windows, plus a hand-written notes footer
docs/FAQ.md                          the only FAQ: exact settings paths, platform limits marked
docs/PRIVACY.md                      what the shipped build does, taken from a dated idle-network capture; no future tense
docs/release-notes/README.md         index: tag, date, Chromium, macOS asset, Windows asset, checksum, errata
docs/release-notes/v*.md             immutable apart from an errata banner at the top
docs/release-notes/v*.checklist.md   the QUALITY release checklist for that tag, required by publish-release
docs/images/readme/                  README captures, always taken from fixture profiles
docs/images/readme/captures.json     file, tag, Chromium, date, tool and fixture for every README capture (social-preview included)
docs/images/                         evidence captures cited by specs and probes, each labelled with the build it measures
docs/perf/                           current measurement records, each naming the build, config and pin it measured
docs/VISION.md                       purpose and non-goals
docs/PRODUCT.md                      the Arc-parity spec, spec only
docs/UI-SPEC.md                      one measured target table, shipped value beside the Arc reference
docs/ROADMAP.md                      milestones as criteria tables: met / not met / no tooling
docs/QUALITY.md                      gates enforced now (each with its command) and targets not yet enforced
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md                 fork strategy and layering, with the measured footprint and its budget
docs/BRAND.md                        voice and trademark hygiene; visuals point to branding/
docs/features/                       one spec per feature; the only place behaviour status lives, machine-checked
docs/decisions/                      ADRs, superseded rather than edited
docs/dev/WORKFLOW.md                 the loop, the one definition of done, the fixup rule by reference, the release procedure
docs/dev/BUILD.md                    the one build guide, for people first: disk, sync, configs, budget, Windows, known failures, numbers with the pin they were measured on
docs/dev/TESTING.md                  filters, interactive tests for input paths, the upstream-suite policy, the reachability audit
docs/dev/TRAPS.md                    rules still true on the current pin, grouped by area, without stories
docs/research/evidence-2026-08.md    market evidence behind the early decisions
docs/research/naming-2026-08.md      the naming record
docs/research/competitors.md         the competitor analysis as a dated snapshot
docs/history/feedback-ledger.md      owner feedback rounds 2 to 10, chronological; never holds open work
docs/history/round6-plan.md          the round 6 plan, trimmed, with a one-paragraph outcome
docs/history/changelog.md            the per-round narrative moved out of AGENTS.md
docs/history/traps-log.md            the dated stories behind the traps
docs/history/backlog-done.md         one line per closed BACKLOG id
docs/history/perf/                   the 2026-09-02 measurements and their README, marked historical
```

#### The patch series

##### Today and the target

Today: 52 patches over 405 files.
- 201 upstream files: +6,401/−522 lines, 437 upstream file×patch touches, 71 upstream files edited by more than one patch (settings_ui.cc by 21 patches, vertical_tab_strip_region_view.cc by 16, prefs_util.cc by 12).
- 204 new files: +28,123 lines.

Target: 18 patches in 6 layers.
- Each upstream file (a path that no patch creates) is owned by exactly one patch.
- The upstream footprint is about 2,000 changed lines in about 130 files.
- No upstream test file is edited.

The order is foundation → model → views → surfaces → platform → branding, and a patch may only use what an earlier patch created.

| Layer | Patch | Owns | Built from today's patches |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 foundation | T01 base | Stedding source roots and wiring: stedding_prefs, registered by one hook each in browser_ui_prefs.cc and browser_prefs.cc; the kArcStyleWindow feature and its params; stedding_strings.grdp (one line in generated_resources.grd); every IDC_STEDDING_* in stedding_command_ids.h; motion and stedding_version; one deps line per upstream target | 02, 10, 16, 17, 18, 29, 35 |
| 0 foundation | T02 defaults | Every upstream default Stedding changes, kept as data. Applied by SetDefaultPrefValue at the end of RegisterProfilePrefs/RegisterLocalState (vertical tabs, restore last session, search suggest off, alternate error pages off, quiet prompts, HTTPS-First balanced, third-party cookies blocked, sign-in promo off), and by entries appended to extra_overrides in ChromeFeatureListCreator::SetUpFieldTrials (the 7 refresh features and GlobalPrivacyControlForce) | 01, 03, 04, 30, 42 |
| 0 foundation | T03 upstream extension points | No Stedding logic and no Stedding includes: the FOLDER collection type and UAF-safe removal order; the hidden-tab predicate and its IsTabHidden call sites; one folder mutation-with-notifications entry point; the session extra-data rebuild registry; the tab_restore entry cap; BrowserWindowFeatures slots; the custom-title hook; the tab-strip API Folder variant | 04, 08, 19, 23, 28, 47 |
| 1 model | T04 spaces | SpaceModel, SpaceRegistry and factory, router, commands, session data, storage partitions, query | 04, 13, 16, 18, 19, 21, 23, 25, 27, 29, 33, 36, 46, 47, 48, 49 (after S-61) |

Dropped outright:
- dead code: MoveTabsToFolderAt / MoveTabsIntoFolderAt, CopyTabToSpace, IsolationMarkForSpace, clipboard_fence and sidebar_file, unless S-61 wires them in the same step;
- the 204 lines of reformatting;
- the capture params in browser_command_controller.cc;
- the edits to upstream tests;
- the Stedding tests living in upstream test files (they move to Stedding test files).

Boundary rules, enforced by `tooling/check-repo patches` and computable from `patches/` alone:
- **B1 Ownership.** An upstream path appears in exactly one patch.
- **B2 Direction.** No patch includes a header, or lists a GN source, that a later patch creates. Today 0001, 0016, 0019 and 0026 fail this.
- **B3 Hooks only.** An upstream hunk adds at most 15 lines, adds no user-visible string literal, does not reformat existing lines (normalised-token check), and adds no history comments (dates, "operator", "round").
- **B4 No upstream test or default edits.** Upstream `*_unittest*` / `*_test*` files are not edited. No FEATURE_*_BY_DEFAULT or Register*Pref default changes in upstream files; those go through T02.
- **B5 Callers.** Every non-test function a patch adds has a non-test caller in the series.
- **B6 Fixes amend their owner.** `git commit --fixup` then autosquash. No round, wave or fixup patches; the README exception is retired.
- **B7 Trailers.** Trailers are parsed with `git interpret-trailers`. Why and Removable-when are non-empty and describe this patch's own content. update-patches writes a generated Upstream-footprint trailer (files, +/− lines). Subjects read `area: summary`, never `feat:`.
