##### Re-cut procedure

Four standing rules apply to every step below.
- **Revert branding first.** Every identity check runs with branding reverted (`tooling/apply-branding --revert`), because branding rewrites about 200 tracked files outside the series. A tree compared with branding applied means nothing.
- **Fixup identity.** Before any autosquash, commit the change on top as `git commit --fixup=<owner>` and record `T=$(git rev-parse HEAD^{tree})`. Run `GIT_SEQUENCE_EDITOR=: git rebase -i --autosquash BASE`, where BASE is the pin commit, the parent of the first patch. Afterwards, `git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}` must equal `T`. If it does not, abort and redo; never patch a lost hunk back by hand. Trap 50 records the one time this was not checked: 97 lines were lost.
- **patches/ agrees with the branch.** After each step, `tooling/update-patches` writes `patches/series.tree`. Then `tooling/apply-patches --check` must rebuild that exact tree from `patches/` alone.
- **Builds are budgeted.** Builds run in chunks the owner approves in advance (the 15-minute rule).

**Phase A: restructure without changing the final tree.** Identity holds by construction, and it is checked at every step anyway.

- **A0 Prepare.**
  1. Revert branding and confirm `git status` is clean.
  2. Tag `stedding/pre-restructure` at the working branch and record `OLD=$(git rev-parse stedding-work)`.
  3. Write `git rev-parse OLD^{tree}` to `patches/series.tree`.
  4. Extend `apply-patches --check` (SER-2) to compare the `git write-tree` of its temporary index with `series.tree`, and to report three-way "merge" verdicts as warnings that need a diff review.

  Check: `apply-patches --check` on today's `patches/` reproduces `series.tree`.
- **A1 Manifest.** `patches/MANIFEST` maps each of the 405 paths in `git diff --name-only BASE OLD` to one target patch. An upstream file goes to the latest layer that any of its hunks needs. Check, by script: the union of the manifest equals the diff's path list, and no path appears twice.
- **A2 Rebuild.** Run `git switch -c stedding-restructure BASE`. For each target patch in order, run `git checkout OLD -- <its paths>`, then `git commit -F <its message>`; the messages are rewritten to rule B7. Check after each commit: `git diff --quiet OLD HEAD -- <its paths>`, meaning the patch's files now equal OLD's.
- **A3 Identity.** `git diff --quiet OLD HEAD` passes, and `git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}` equals `patches/series.tree`. A failure means the manifest missed a path: fix MANIFEST and return to A2.
- **A4 Bisectability.** The B2 detector reports nothing on any of the 18 commits. Then run `gn gen` and an incremental build at each of the 6 layer boundaries. To fix a failure, move a path to a later patch in MANIFEST and repeat A2 and A3; the final tree does not change, and A3 proves it after every move.
- **A5 Switch over.**
  1. Keep the old branch as `stedding-work-old`, and rename `stedding-restructure` to `stedding-work`.
  2. Run `tooling/update-patches` and `tooling/check-repo patches`.
  3. Run `tooling/apply-patches --check`; it must reproduce `series.tree`.
  4. Make one project commit holding `patches/`, `MANIFEST` and `series.tree`.

**Phase B: shrink the upstream footprint, one hotspot per step.** Here the tree changes on purpose, so tests hold the behaviour and identity checks surround the change. Each step:
1. Before: `apply-patches --check` reproduces `series.tree`, so `patches/` and the branch agree.
2. Add the hook and the Stedding-owned file, delete the logic from the upstream file, and commit with `--fixup=<owning target patch>`.
3. Autosquash under the fixup identity rule above.
4. `git diff --stat <before> HEAD` lists only this step's upstream files and its new Stedding files.
5. Build. Run the feature suites, the upstream suites around the file (trap 31) and the interactive tests (REPO-2, and the tab-selection test from the bug work). Take the spec's captures.
6. Run `update-patches`; `apply-patches --check` reproduces the new `series.tree`; the footprint table in `patches/README.md` shows the file's upstream lines going down.

The steps, in order:
- **B-a** Revert the format-only hunks. For every reverted hunk, the normalised-token check shows the removed and added text are equal, which proves there is no semantic change; the build still runs.
- **B-b** Route defaults through the two T02 hooks. Revert the in-place flips and the upstream test edits they forced, and move the Stedding tests out of upstream test files.
- **B-c** The settings_ui.cc and prefs_util.cc hubs.
- **B-d** Command controller, accelerators, menus and command ids.
- **B-e** SidebarController and TabRowDecorator: the region view, tab_view, tab_strip_view, the layouts, toolbar, location bar and the downloads controller.
- **B-f** Folder operations out of TabStripModel and TabStripCollection.
- **B-g** Platform files: install modes, profile paths, the app controller, and removal of the Android edits. The keychain fix will already have landed (SER-1).
- **B-h** Wire or withdraw the dead code (S-61).

**Phase C: CI.** `series.yml` runs B1–B7 and the `series.tree` comparison on every change to `patches/`.

#### Work, in order

Docs and layout. The two P0 items come first because every later item depends on them. Items that correct what users read are P1 and land before the next release. The restructure of internal docs is P2.

**Why the series re-cut waits for the P0/P1 bug work.** The re-cut is P2. Phase A must not start until the P0/P1 fixes have landed and been released, and until no fixup is in flight and no pin move is under way. There are four reasons:
- The worst bugs, tab selection and the crashes, live in the files Phase B rewrites most: tab_view.cc, the region view and tab_strip_model.cc. A small fixup on today's series keeps the fix reviewable and bisectable. The same fix made during the re-cut would be buried inside a 700-line move.
- Phase B changes behaviour-bearing code. It is safe only once tests hold that behaviour: the interactive tab-click test, the four fixtures no sweep runs today (REPO-2), and upstream suites that are no longer edited. Those tests arrive with the P0/P1 work.
- Every P0/P1 fix lands as a fixup into today's patches. A concurrent re-cut moves every patch boundary, so each in-flight fixup would need to be re-targeted by hand. That is exactly the kind of conflict resolution that has already lost code (trap 50).
- Users are on beta 8 (M153), behind stable, while fixes wait: patch 0050, and a signed build. The re-cut costs days, with a build at each of six layer boundaries, and the release comes first.

Patch series:

- [ ] **SER-1** (P0) Fix the keychain legacy fallback now, as a fixup into 0038 (T15 after the re-cut).
  - Fall back to "Chromium Safe Storage" only for a proven legacy profile, using the marker PLT-1 adds. Patch 0050 has no marker today: it only checks whether Stedding's folder exists. So this gate lands with PLT-1, as section 3's Phase 0 describes.
  - After one successful legacy read, copy the key into the Stedding item, so that later launches never touch Chromium's item.

  Fixes: SER-F2. Done when: unit tests show that (a) a fresh profile, with a Chromium item present and no legacy marker, never reads it, (b) a legacy profile reads it once and writes the Stedding item, and (c) a second launch reads only the Stedding item; the autosquash leaves `HEAD^{tree}` equal to the recorded tree; WEB-1's and PLT-2's checks pass on a real Mac.
- [ ] **SER-2** (P1) Build the series safety tools before more fixups land.
  - `patches/series.tree` is written by update-patches and compared by `apply-patches --check`; three-way "merge" verdicts are reported as warnings.
  - check-repo and update-patches parse trailers with `git interpret-trailers --parse` and require non-empty, line-start Why and Removable-when.
  - The B2 forward-reference detector lives in `check-repo patches`. It reports today's four cases as known until SER-4.

  Fixes: SER-F1, SER-F11, SER-F12. Done when: `apply-patches --check` on a clean checkout prints the tree match; a patch with "Why:" only inside its diff fails; the detector lists exactly 0001, 0016, 0019 and 0026.

Add a test for each, as a fixup into the mac platform patch. Or, if one cannot be patched, supersede ADR 0009 and state the user impact in BACKLOG. Fixes: DOC-I3. Done when: none of the three files compares against or embeds org.chromium.Chromium for a Stedding build; each has a test asserting the Stedding id; ADR 0009 names the identifier each of the three sites now uses, or a newer ADR supersedes it and BACKLOG states the user impact.

Done when: `git diff --quiet OLD HEAD`; `HEAD^{tree}` equals `patches/series.tree`; B1 and B2 report nothing; all six layer boundaries build; `apply-patches --check` rebuilds the tree from `patches/` alone.

## Appendix: method

**Scope and units.** The audit covered all 405 files the series changes, 34.5k lines, split into 16 code review units. Each unit had two independent review passes. Each pass read its unit through a correctness lens (lifetimes, invariants, reachable crashes) and through a product lens (does the feature do what its spec row and its user expect). Together they filed about 1,200 reports. Four more audits ran beside them: the tooling, the docs (both the pages people read and the internal ones), every feature-spec row, and the patch series.

**Merging and verification.** Reports of medium or higher severity were merged by root cause into clusters. Twenty-two verifiers then checked each cluster against the code at HEAD. Low-severity reports were not verified; they appear in each area's "Low-severity findings (not verified)" table as leads.

**What the states mean.**

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| confirmed | A verifier traced the failure to specific lines at HEAD and followed the path a user takes to reach it. Not reproduced: nothing was built or run. |
| plausible | The mechanism is real in the code, but whether a user can reach it, or what it causes, is not settled. Confirm it dynamically before fixing it (section 2). |
| refuted | A verifier showed the claim is wrong. The claim was dropped; see the list below. |
| unchecked | A cluster no verifier reached. There are none. |
| not verified | Low-severity reports (the `-L` rows). Leads, not findings. |

**Counts.**

| | Number |
|---|---|
| Reports filed by the review passes | about 1,200 |
| Root-cause clusters (medium or higher when reported) | 359 |
| Refuted clusters, and the reports they had merged | 6, merging 9 |
| Unchecked clusters | 0 |
| Standing clusters | 353: 332 confirmed, 21 plausible |
| Standing clusters by severity after verification | 24 critical, 91 high, 210 medium, 28 low |
| Low-severity reports, not verified | 260 |
| Tooling findings (`PLT-T` rows) | 25 |

| Area | Prefix | Clusters | Refuted | Critical | High | Medium | Low | Plausible |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tabs, folders and the sidebar | TAB | 69 | 0 | 6 | 22 | 37 | 4 | 5 |
| Spaces | SPC | 98 | 1 | 10 | 28 | 56 | 3 | 7 |
| Window, toolbar and page | WIN | 38 | 0 | 1 | 4 | 23 | 10 | 3 |
| Command bar, commands, menus, peek and links | CMD | 69 | 2 | 4 | 19 | 39 | 5 | 3 |
| Settings, welcome, archive and import | WEB | 75 | 3 | 2 | 17 | 49 | 4 | 3 |
| Platform, tooling and release | PLT | 10 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 6 | 2 | 0 |

**Refuted claims.** These show that the verification also killed wrong claims.

- SPC: "SetActiveSpace release-CHECKs on an unknown Space id." None of the holders cited can outlive a deletion made elsewhere. For example, the command bar closes on any focus change.
- CMD: "Action rows bind a raw target window and Space ids captured when the list is built." Unreachable at HEAD. The "Open in" rows are built only in a little window, and the bar never opens in one. The "Move Tab to" list lives only while the bar is open.
- CMD: "The 'Open in <Space>' rows hold the target window with base::Unretained." Unreachable at HEAD for the same reason. Only three commands open the bar, and none of them in a little window.
- WEB: "Deleting a Space that is active in another window reassigns that window's active Space without OnActiveSpaceChanged." The visible failure does not happen, because the visibility controller updates the rows and the tint on OnSpaceRemoved.
- WEB: "Esc closes the welcome without setting kWelcomeShown, so it reappears." While the page is alive, the dialog's WebView does not let Esc through as an accelerator, so that path does not exist.
- WEB: "A real first launch shows Chromium's profile-picker first-run flow before the welcome." Shipped builds have no Google OAuth client or API keys, so that flow does not start.

**What was not covered, and why.**

- **Nothing was executed.** No build, no test run, no capture. Every finding is a reading of the code, including the crashes. That is why each item's "Done when" is checked on a real build and plausible findings are reproduced first.
- **Windows-only code was read, not exercised.** Findings about the Windows installer, identity and keyboard map were made without a Windows build.
- **The branding output was not reviewed line by line.** `tooling/apply-branding` rewrites about 200 tracked files, and the rewritten text was not read file by file.
- **The tests and repository sections, and the `PLT-T` tooling rows, are first-pass work and were not re-verified.** Their verdicts on individual tests, spec rows, docs and patches are claims to check before acting on them.
- **The owner's click bug was not reproduced.** The reading narrows its causes, and TAB-1 and TST-1 are how it gets settled.
